    See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Bereshit* 26; *Bereshit Rabbah* 20:11; BT *Eruvin* 18b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-9b|1:9b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34b|34b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54b|54b–55a]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-231b|2:231b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-76b|3:76b]].

[^85]: ***Toward evening he shall bathe in water*** The verse continues: *and when the sun sets, he may come inside the camp*.

[^86]: **flow, leprosy, or menstrual defilement** Genital discharge, skin disease, or contact with a menstruous woman.

[^87]: **that immersion is insufficient…** He has to wait seven days and then immerse himself again. See M *Berakhot* 3:6; BT *Berakhot* 21b, 26a; JT *Shevu’ot* 2:1, 33d; *Zohorei Ya’bets*.

[^88]: ***the One*** That is, God.

[^89]: ***Pure*—Abraham who issued from Terah** See *Bemidbar Rabbah* 19:1: “*Who can bring forth pure from impure? Is it not the One?* For example, Abraham from Terah, Hezekiah from Ahaz, Josiah from Amon, Mordecai from Shim’i, Israel from the idolaters, the world to come from this world. Who did this? Who commanded this? Who decreed this? Was it not the Unique One of the world?” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-48b|3:48b]], [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-180b|180b]].

[^90]: **rung of Abraham. Who is that? Water** *Ḥesed*, the flowing love of God, symbolized by Abraham.

    See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 12; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|1: 77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^91]: **warned him** Urging him to reject idolatry.

[^92]: **If not** If the impurity was more severe.

[^93]: **for seven** For seven days.

[^94]: **in order to see and test them** Before he realized they were angels.

[^95]: **the blessed Holy One, who is the Tree of Life for all** *Tif’eret*, symbolized by the Tree of Life.

[^96]: **When Adam sinned…** The Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil symbolizes *Shekhinah*. Adam’s sin was that he worshiped and partook of *Shekninah* alone, splitting Her off from the other *sefirot* and divorcing Her from Her husband, *Tif’eret*, the Tree of Life.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-12b|1:12b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|35b–36a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-53b|53b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z1-221a|221a–b]]; Scholem, *Major Trends*, 231–32, 236, 404–5, n. 105; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:373–76.

[^97]: **he mended the world with another tree, the Tree of Life** Mending the damage inflicted by Adam. See *Ba’al ha-Turim* on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-20|Genesis 9:20]].

[^98]: ***I will surely return to you when life is due*** The verse continues: *and behold, your wife Sarah will have a son!*

[^99]: **The verse should read: *He will surely return*** Referring to God, not to the angel speaking with Abraham.

[^100]: **‘Three keys have not been handed over to any messenger…’** See BT *Ta’anit* 2a: “Rabbi Yoḥanan said, ‘Three keys lie in the hands of the blessed Holy One and have not been handed over to any messenger; namely, the key of rain, the key of childbirth, and the key of resurrection.’”

[^101]: ***He said*, anonymously, or *He called*, anonymously** Without naming the divine subject. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-115a|1:115a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138a|138a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|142b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-60b|2:60b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-125b|125b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-131a|131a]].

[^102]: **Angel of Covenant** *Shekhinah*, who joins with *Yesod*, the divine phallus, known as Covenant. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-131b|1:131b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-135b|135b]].

[^103]: **entirely the blessed Holy One** Entirely divine.

[^104]: **that he would issue from Hagar, as previously** That the son-to-be would issue from Hagar, as had Ishmael.

[^105]: **When he bound him on the altar** When Abraham bound Isaac.

[^106]: **Isaac was thirty-seven years old… if he had kicked**… See *Seder Eliyyahu Zuta* 2: “Isaac… said to his father, ‘Father, bind me well and place me on the altar, lest I kick you and smite you, and find myself doubly guilty of murder. I am a young man, in full strength, thirty-seven years old!’”

    On Isaac’s age at the time of his ordeal, see *Seder Olam Rabbah* 1; *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 24; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 23; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 31; Theodor’s note on *Bereshit Rabbah* 55:4, 587–88.

[^107]: **he honored his father, allowing himself to be bound**… See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 31: “Isaac said to his father Abraham, ‘Father, bind me! Bind both my hands and both my feet… \[lest\] I violate *Honor your father* ([[Torah 17 - Yitro#^20-12|Exodus 20:12]]).’” See David Luria, ad loc., n. 36; *Yalqut Shim’oni*, Deuteronomy, 830.

[^108]: ***And a servant his master*—Eliezer to Abraham** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-146b|1:146b]].

[^109]: **quite handsome** According to *Bereshit Rabbah* 59:8, Eli’ezer resembled Abraham. According to *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 16, Eli’ezer was a prince, son of King Nimrod (according to another version, Nimrod’s servant), presented by the king to Abraham after the latter emerged unscathed from the fiery furnace. According to *Soferim*, add. 1:2, Eli’ezer was none other than the giant Og, on whose beauty see Josephus, *Antiquities of the Jews* 4:5:3.

[^110]: ***If I am a father, where is My honor?*** The verse continues: *If I am a master, where is My reverence?*

[^111]: ***Behold, a son!*** The verse may be rendered: *Behold, a son for your wife Sarah!* or, as previously: *Behold, your wife Sarah will have a son!*

[^112]: **Sarah’s son, because of whom she died…** According to midrashic tradition, Sarah died in anguish over Isaac’s ordeal on the altar.

    See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 20:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 26:3; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 23; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 32; *Bereshit Rabbah* 58:5; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 05 - Chayei Sara#^23-2|Genesis 23:2]].

[^113]: **ascending because of him, when the blessed Holy One sits…** According to rabbinic tradition, Sarah conceived on Rosh Hashanah, the Day of Judgment. Her memory is invoked and her miracle commemorated by the Scriptural reading for the first day of the festival, which begins: *YHVH attended to Sarah as He had said*. The reading for the second day recounts the binding of Isaac, recalling his merit as God sits in judgment over the world. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 73:1; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 17; BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 10b–11a.

[^114]: **female absorbs the son from male** According to medieval science, the female receives the seed from the male.

[^115]: ***and he was behind it*** The phrase is usually rendered, *which was behind him*, referring to the opening of the tent behind the messenger, but Rabbi Shim’on understands it differently.

[^116]: **The verse should read:… *and she was behind him*** Referring to Sarah, who was behind the messenger (or behind Abraham). The word אחריו (*aḥarav*) can mean “behind it” or “behind him.”

[^117]: ***Sarah heard* what was spoken by *the opening of the tent*…** The verse reads: האהל‎ פתח‎ שומעת‎ ושרה (*Ve-Sarah shoma’at petaḥ haohel*), usually rendered: *Sarah was listening at the opening of the tent*. The Hebrew, however, employs no preposition before *the opening*. Taking advantage of this ellipsis, Rabbi Shim’on reads the words hyperliterally: *Sarah heard the opening of the tent*. She heard *Shekhinah*, the opening of the divine realm. See above, pages 119–20. Here “the blessed Holy One” refers to *Shekhinah*.

[^118]: ***ve-hu, and He*, spoke *aḥarav, after it*** The divine male, *Tif’eret*, spoke after *the opening of the tent, Shekhinah*.

[^119]: **Alternatively, Sarah was sitting *at the opening of the tent* to listen in** Understanding *the opening of the tent* according to its plain meaning.

[^120]: **The span of days befitting them…** Their days on earth correspond to the cosmic days: the *sefirot*. Abraham’s one hundred years correspond to the full sefirotic decade, while Sarah’s ninety years correspond to nine *sefirot*.

[^121]: **They had entered days** Rabbi Shim’on reads the phrase בימים‎ באים (*ba’im ba-yamim*), *advanced in days*, hyperliterally: *coming into days*, entering the sefirotic days. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.07 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-126a|1:126a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-129b|129b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142a|142a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-224a|224a]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-170b|3:170b]].

[^122]: ***Advanced in days*, as in the expression ‘The day has advanced’…** This stage of their lives was drawing to a close. Now, with the announcement of Isaac’s birth, a new phase beckoned.

[^123]: **delight, menstruating…** עדונא (*Idduna*), implying both “delight” and “menstrual period.” Though past menopause, Sarah miraculously rejuvenated.

    See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-12|Genesis 18:12]]: *Sarah laughed within, saying: “After I have withered, am I to have* עדנה (*ednah*), *delight—with my husband so old?”* Rabbinic midrash transforms Sarah’s rhetorical question into a declarative statement. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:17: “*After I have withered, I have had ednah!*… A woman, as long as she is young, has menstrual periods, while as for me, *after I have withered, I have had ednah*, menstrual periods, but *my husband is old!*”

    See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 36: “That moment, as Sarah was kneading \[dough to prepare cakes for the messengers\], she saw *the way of women* \[i.e., she discovered herself menstruating\].”

    See BT *Bava Metsi’a* 87a: “*After I have withered, I have had ednah!* Rav Ḥisda said, ‘After the flesh had withered and wrinkles multiplied, the flesh נתעדן (*nitadden*), “rejuvenated, became tender,” the wrinkles were smoothed out, and beauty returned to its site.’”

[^124]: ***Your glory is above the heavens*** The verse reads: *His glory is above the heavens*.

[^125]: **Finally all… declare: *Blessed be the glory of YHVH from His place!*** Wherever He is. See BT *Ḥagigah* 13b: “Behold it is written: *Blessed be the glory of YHVH from His place!*—implying that no one knows His place.”

    Cf. *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 4; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-159a|3:159a]]; and *Bahir* 90 (131): “*Blessed be the glory of YHVH from its place!* What is *the glory of YHVH?* A parable. To what can this be compared? To a king who had a matron in his chamber, with whom all his soldiers delighted. She had children who came each day to see the king and praise him. They asked him, ‘Where is our mother?’ He replied, ‘You cannot see her now.’ They said, ‘May she be blessed wherever she is!’”

[^126]: **But indeed, *Her husband is known in the gates*—the blessed Holy One** The biblical description of the “woman of valor” ([[Proverbs 31#^31-10|Proverbs 31:10–31]]) is understood in Kabbalah as a hymn to *Shekhinah*. Her husband is the blessed Holy One. See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 11:8.

[^127]: **opens the gates of imagination** Aramaic, משער (*mesha’er*), “estimates, imagines,” playing on the Hebrew שער (*sha’ar*), “gate.” Imagination enables the human mind to fathom God—though, as Rabbi Yehudah goes on to say, all imaginative representations fall short of true divine being. See the thirteenth-century German Hasidic *Shir ha-Kavod:* “They have portrayed You in many visions; You remain one through all imaginings.”

    On the relation between visions of the divine and human imagination, see Hai Gaon (cited in *Arukh*, s.v., *avnei shayish tahor*), describing the ascent of the Merkavah mystics: “Not that they actually ascend on high, but rather they see and gaze within the recesses of their minds.” Ḥananel son of Ḥushiel (on BT *Berakhot* 6a, cited by Judah ben Barzillai, *Peirush Sefer Yetsirah*, 32) writes: “The blessed Holy One reveals His glory to those who revere and love Him through דלבא‎ אובנתא (*uvneta de-libba*), apprehension \[or: projections\] of the mind.”

    See BT *Megillah* 24b; *Otsar ha-Ge’onim* 1, appendix, 3; *NZ*, ad loc., n. 1; Wolfson, *Through a Speculum That Shines*, 144–48.

[^128]: ***Lift up your heads, O gates! Be lifted up, openings of eternity!*** The verse continues: *So the King of Glory may come*.

[^129]: **Through these gates, supernal rungs…** Through the *sefirot*, the Unknown becomes known. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|1:160b]]; 3:253, 253b (*RM*).

[^130]: **smooth members** שייפין (*Shaifin*), from a root meaning “to smooth, rub, slip.” In the *Zohar*, the word signifies “limbs,” perhaps based on the Talmudic expression (BT *Sotah* 7b) לשפא‎ איבריה‎ על (*al eivreih le-shafa*), “each limb entered its socket”—“slipping” into place—or “…the chest.”

    See *Arukh*, s.v. *shaf*; Rashi, ad loc., and on [[Job 33#^33-21|Job 33:21]], citing *Ḥullin* 42b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-170a|3:170a]].

[^131]: **soul of soul** The human soul originates in God, who is the essence of this essence. The phrase “soul of soul” derives from Solomon ibn Gabirol’s *Keter Malkhut* 4:47: “You are alive but not through… soul, for You are soul of soul.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245a|1:245a]]; [[Zohar 3.109b|3:109b]] (*RM*), [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-152a|152a]]; Scholem, *Major Trends*, 110, 375, n. 97. On the parallel between the soul and God, see BT *Berakhot* 10a; *Midrash Tehillim* 103:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140a|1:140a]].

[^132]: **openings for soul** The *sefirot* are openings for the human soul to approach the hidden God, and openings for “soul of soul” to manifest.

[^133]: **There is opening within opening, rung upon rung…** The *sefirot* are revealed one by one, as the seeker journeys within and beyond.

[^134]: ***Opening of the tent*—opening of Righteousness…** *Shekhinah* is the opening of the divine realm and is known as Righteousness. See above, pages 119–20, 131–32.

[^135]: **all other supernal openings come into view** The other *sefirot* are reflected in *Shekhinah*.

[^136]: **Now—when this opening is unknown, since Israel is in exile…** Israel’s exile symbolizes cosmic dysfunction, the rupturing of sefirotic unity. The higher openings have closed shut and vanished, leaving *Shekhinah* Herself in exile: abandoned by the higher *sefirot* and unknown on earth.

[^137]: **they cannot know…** Israel cannot know.

[^138]: ***spirit of wisdom and understanding*…** The various spirits in the verse represent higher *sefirot*.

[^139]: **All those are destined to alight upon King Messiah below** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-130b|3:130b]] (*IR*).

[^140]: ***with righteousness*** With *Shekhinah*, who includes all the *sefirot*.

[^141]: **Abraham was gladdened** With the news that a son would be born to Sarah.

[^142]: ***He said*—who it was is not recorded. It was *opening of the tent*** The anonymous subject is none other than *Shekhinah*. See above, pages 129–30.

[^143]: ***Sarah heard the opening of the tent*** She heard *Shekhinah*. See above, note 117.

[^144]: ***whose seed is in it, according to its kind*** Isaac issued from Abraham as holy seed.

[^145]: **that seed of his was unholy** Ishmael.

[^146]: **foreskin below** The demonic realm. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18a|18a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|35b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (68).

[^147]: **engendering above** Engendering Isaac, who symbolizes *Gevurah*. Alternatively, the phrase may refer to Isaac’s engendering of Jacob, who symbolizes *Tif’eret*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|1:96a]].

[^148]: **Abraham cleaved to his rung fittingly** Now Abraham attained fully the *sefirah* of *Ḥesed*. See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^149]: **Water actually conceived and bore darkness** The flowing love of *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham and water, engendered the rigor of *Gevurah*, symbolized by Isaac and darkness. See *Shemot Rabbah* 15:22: “Water conceived and bore darkness.” Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32b|1:32b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142a|142a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-168b|168b]].

[^150]: **Why did the blessed Holy One call him יצחק (*Yitsḥaq*), Isaac…?** The name יצחק (*Yitsḥaq*) means “He laughs.” See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-12|Genesis 18:12–15]]; [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-6|21:6]].

[^151]: **Apparently He called him Isaac before he issued into the world** Whereas normally the mother or father names the child at birth.

[^152]: **fire appropriates water, for water flows on the side of *Gevurah*** *Gevurah* (on the left, symbolized by fire and by Isaac) encompasses *Ḥesed* (on the right, symbolized by water and by Abraham) and conveys it below. See *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:12.

[^153]: **Come and ask the Levites, who gladden that side…** The Levites, deriving from *Gevurah*, sweeten the harshness of the left side. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|1:142b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-148b|148b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-48b|3:48b]], [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-121b|121b]] (*RM*).

[^154]: **So Isaac is joy, issuing from that side and cleaving there** Isaac, whose name derives from “laughter,” is linked with *Gevurah*, who is gladdened.

[^155]: ***Yitsḥaq*, Isaac—jesting, joy, transforming…** Isaac symbolizes the transformation of *Ḥesed* into *Gevurah* and their dialectical interaction, which engenders joy. The interplay of these two *sefirot* is essential: too much *Ḥesed* would generate indulgence; too much *Gevurah* (also called *Din*, severe “Judgment”) would doom the world. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-118b|1:118b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-119b|119b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|133b]].

[^156]: **Therefore he was named so…** Isaac’s name is not merely personal; it symbolizes a vital cosmic principle.

[^157]: **announcing it to Abraham** By telling him: *You will name him Isaac* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-19|Genesis 17:19]]).

[^158]: **In all other cases, the blessed Holy One let them name their children…** Other parents were allowed to select a name for their children.

[^159]: **you and no one else, transforming…** Abraham, symbolizing *Ḥesed*, had to name his son Isaac, symbolizing *Gevurah*, in order to harmonize both *sefirot*. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-118a|1:118a]]–b.

[^160]: ***The men arose from there and gazed toward Sodom*** The verse continues: —*Abraham walking with them to send them off*. Now that *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah* had been harmonized, the angels could proceed to Sodom. The city would be destroyed through the harsh rigor of *Gevurah*, while Lot would be saved through the quality of *Ḥesed*.

[^161]: **He sends him a gift What is that? A poor person…** So he can demonstrate virtue by helping him. See *Sefer ha-Yashar* (attributed to Rabbeinu Tam), chap. 13: “As the sage said, ‘When the Creator wishes to send a gift to his devotees, he arranges for a poor person to appear at his gate.’” Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-198a|2:198a]].

[^162]: **thread emanating from the right side** From *Ḥesed*. Cf. the rabbinic phrase “a thread of *Ḥesed*” (BT *Megillah* 13a, *Ḥagigah* 12b). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-76a|3:76a]].

[^163]: **he withdraws from him** The Destroyer withdraws from God’s beloved.

[^164]: ***God remembered Abraham*… What did He remember?…** Abraham’s hospitality toward the three angels gained him merit, on account of which God saved his nephew Lot.

    See *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^23-5|Deuteronomy 23:5]]: “I sent Lot, their \[the Moabites’\] ancestor *out of the midst of the upheaval* only on account of Abraham’s merit, as is said: *So it was, when God destroyed the cities of the plain, that God remembered Abraham and sent Lot out* \[*of the midst of the upheaval*\].”

    Cf. *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:38; *Tanḥuma, Balaq* 12; *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^13-5|Genesis 13:5]]).

[^165]: ***Charity delivers from death*** See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 16b; *Bava Batra* 10a.

[^166]: **the blessed Holy One provided Abraham an opportunity in advance** By sending him the three messengers.

[^167]: **although they were angels** Who require no food.

[^168]: ***They ate*, eaten by their fire!** By angelic fire. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:14; BT *Bava Metsi’a* 86b; *Da’at Zeqenim* (*Ba’alei ha-Tosafot*) on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-8|Genesis 18:8]]; *Zohar* [[#^z1-101b|1:101b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-102a|102a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-144a|144a]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:236, n. 143.

[^169]: **three angels: this one, fire; this one, water; this one, air!** The three angels are identified in BT *Bava Metsi’a* 86b, as Michael, Gabriel, and Raphael. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-98b|1:98b]]–99a (*ST*). Here they are associated respectively with water, fire, and air. The question is: How could all the angels have consumed the food by fire, if only one of them (Gabriel) was fiery?

[^170]: **But each encompassed the other** Each of the three included all three elements.

[^171]: **actually eating, feasting on *Shekhinah*!** See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 20:10: “Rabbi Hosha’ya said, ‘Did they \[the nobles of Israel\] take provisions up with them to Sinai, that it is written: *They beheld God* \[*and they ate and drank*\]? Rather, this teaches that they feasted their eyes on *Shekhinah*, like a person looking at his friend while eating and drinking.’ Rabbi Yoḥanan said, ‘Actual eating, as is said: *In the light of the king’s face is life* ([[Proverbs 16#^16-15|Proverbs 16:15]]).’”

    Here Rabbi El’azar blends these two contrasting interpretations: The nobles of Israel enjoyed true nourishment, feasting on *Shekhinah*. A similar blend is offered by Ezra of Gerona (in Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 15) and Jacob ben Sheshet, *Sefer ha-Emunah ve-ha-Bittaḥon*, 377. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-126a|2:126a]].

[^172]: **they generated nourishment from the side of Abraham…** By consuming the food offered by Abraham, the angels generated a nourishing flow of emanation from his *sefirah, Ḥesed*. According to Rabbi Yitsḥak (*Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana*, 6:1; *Tanḥuma, Pinḥas* 12), angels “are nourished from the splendor of *Shekhinah.”* See above, page 127.

[^173]: **one should drink from the cup of blessing to attain blessing…** By drinking from a cup of wine at the conclusion of the blessing after meals, one stimulates a flow of blessing from above. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-250a|1:250a]].

[^174]: **to deliver Lot** From Sodom.

[^175]: ***Gaze from Your holy abode*** The verse continues: —*from heaven—and bless Your people Israel*.

[^176]: **Just as there, compassion, so here, compassion** Rabbi El’azar overturns the interpretation of Rabbi Alexandri (*Tanḥuma*, *Ki Tissa* 14, adopted by Rashi on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-16|Genesis 18:16]]), according to whom the verb השקיף (*hishqif*), “to look down, gaze,” always denotes suffering, except in [[Torah 50 - Ki Tavo#^26-15|Deuteronomy 26:15]].

[^177]: ***to send them off*—to escort them** See *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ, Petiḥta; Tanḥuma, Beshallaḥ* 4; Rashi, ad loc.

[^178]: **why did he escort them?** Surely, angels need no escort.

[^179]: ***And YHVH said, ‘Shall I hide from Abraham*** The verse continues: *what I am about to do?’*

[^180]: **to join him** To join the other.

[^181]: **So, *And YHVH said*** Because Abraham escorted his guests, God revealed Himself.

[^182]: **Whatever He does… He does by means of the righteous…** With their assent or cooperation. See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 6: “Even when the blessed Holy One grew angry over Sodom because of their wicked deeds and sought to overturn Sodom, He did not seal their decree of punishment before conferring with Abraham, as is said: *And YHVH said, ‘Shall I hide from Abraham* \[*what I am about to do*\]?’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:2; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 5–6; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 7; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-104b|1:104b]] (*MhN*).

[^183]: **so they will have no pretext…** In order that the sinners will have no pretext, God divulges His secret to the righteous.

[^184]: **Furthermore, so they cannot claim…** So the wicked cannot claim.

[^185]: ***You will be clear before YHVH and before Israel*** See M *Sheqalim* 3:2.

[^186]: **How?** How can sinners protect themselves?

[^187]: **returning** Turning back to God.

[^188]: ***Raise your eyes now and see*** The verse continues: *from the place where you are, to the north and south and east and west*.

[^189]: **these places** Sodom and Gomorrah.

[^190]: **I have already given the land to Abraham, so I must inform him…** See *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 5: “Why did the blessed Holy One reveal this to Abraham? Rabbi Yehudah son of Levi said, ‘A parable: A king had an orchard. He gave it to his beloved as a gift. Sometime later, the king had to cut down five of its tree trunks. He said, “Although it is mine, I gave it to my beloved as a gift, so it isn’t right to cut down anything until I consult with him.” Similarly, when Abraham went up to the land of Israel, the blessed Holy One said to him, “*Raise your eyes now and see from the place where you are, to the north and south and east and west, for all the land that you see I give to you.”* Therefore when He sought to destroy these five cities \[see *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:4\], He said, “I am not going to destroy them without his knowledge.”… So when He was about to destroy them He consulted with Abraham, as is said: *And YHVH said, “Shall I hide* \[*from Abraham what I am about to do?*”\].’”

    See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 7; *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:2.

[^191]: **he did not plead with Him to save Lot from the punishment** See *Zohar* [[#^z1-111a|1:111a]]. According to *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25, at this point Abraham did plead for Lot. See David Luria, ad loc., n. 8.

[^192]: **So as not to demand reward for his deeds** Abraham was too humble to demand that God save Lot on account of Abraham’s own righteousness.

[^193]: **for Abraham’s sake…** By virtue of Abraham’s humility, without his having to ask. See above, note 164.

[^194]: **What does this mean: *where Lot dwelled?*** Was he the only one living in those cities?

[^195]: **except for Lot** So all the cities were inhabited only for his sake.

[^196]: **Now, did Lot dwell in all of them?** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:6; *Sekhel Tov*, ad loc.; below, page 155.

[^197]: **he learns from his ways…** The one serving learns from the ways of the righteous person.

[^198]: **he learned to render kindness to creatures…** As when he welcomed the angels into his home in Sodom. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-1|Genesis 19:1–3]]; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 11; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 15; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-78b|1:78b]].

[^199]: **the blessed Holy One appeared to him on this rung …*El Shaddai*** *Tif’eret*, known as “the blessed Holy One,” manifested to Abraham through *Shekhinah*, known as *El Shaddai* (“God of *Shaddai*”). *Shaddai* is an obscure divine name, which may mean “of the mountain.” The traditional rendering “Almighty God” is unjustified.

[^200]: **First, אמר‎ ויהוה (*Va-YHVH amar*), *And YHVH said*** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:2: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Wherever it is said: *And YHVH*, this implies: He and His Court.’” In Kabbalah this court symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment”) and pronounces the divine decree, so the phrase *And YHVH* encompasses “He \[*Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*\] and His Court \[*Shekhinah*\]*.”* Here Rabbi Shim’on implies that at first both these *sefirot* spoke to Abraham.

    See JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b; Rashi on [[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-29|Exodus 12:29]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64b|64b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-107b|107b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|2:37b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-44b|44b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-149a|3:149a]]. The hermeneutical significance of *and* was championed by Rabbi Akiva. See BT *Yevamot* 68b; *Sanhedrin* 51b.

[^201]: **then, יהוה‎ ויאמר (*Va-yomer YHVH*)*, YHVH said*…** Then *Tif’eret* spoke alone, but still manifesting through *Shekhinah*.

[^202]: **כלה‎ עשו (*asu kalah*), *carry out annihilation*!** Rabbi Shim’on understands the phrase as a command, though its plain meaning is declarative. The verse is often understood as: *I will go down and see if asu kalah, they have done altogether, according to the cry that has come to Me*. See *Zohorei Ya’bets*.

[^203]: **speaking with one and commanding another?** Speaking with Abraham, yet commanding the angels.

[^204]: **in whose jurisdiction those places lay** Sodom and Gomorrah were part of the territory given by God to Abraham, so before destroying them He sought Abraham’s approval. See *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 5; above, page 140.

[^205]: **Because of Abraham and *Shekhinah*, who did not depart from him** *YHVH* sought the approval of both.

[^206]: **Alternatively, *asu*, to do, according to its translation: *they have done*** Understanding the verb as it is rendered in *Targum Onqelos:* עבדו (*avadu*), *they have done*. The verse would then mean: *I will go down and see if they have done altogether according to the cry that has come to Me*. See above, note 202.

[^207]: **But didn’t the blessed Holy One know?…** How the inhabitants of Sodom and Gomorrah had acted. See *Sekhel Tov*, ad loc: “Even though it is revealed before Me that it is so, I must consider their case in the presence of witnesses: Gabriel, Michael, and you, Lot.”

[^208]: **from the rung of compassion to the rung of judgment…** When human wickedness demands judgment and punishment, this entails God’s descent from the heights of compassion. See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-20|Genesis 18:20]]; cf. JT *Ta’anit* 2:1, 65b; Maimonides, *Guide of the Perplexed* 1:10.

[^209]: ***God saw the Children of Israel, and He knew*** He saw their suffering in Egypt and took note of them.

[^210]: **Therefore the blessed Holy One said, *Shall I hide from Abraham?*** Because He sought his consent.

[^211]: **even when the blessed Holy One sits in judgment over the world** As here, over Sodom and Gomorrah.

[^212]: **He does not change** His compassionate nature.

[^213]: **That applies to an individual, this to the community…** God is accessible to an individual or in a particular location only at certain times, but is always accessible to the community or in the world as a whole. See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 18a.

[^214]: **בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*)… באברהם (*be-Avraham*)…** According to Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Korḥah (*Bereshit Rabbah* 12:9), בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*), *when they were created*, is an anagram of באברהם (*be-Avraham*), “through Abraham,” indicating that the world was created for his sake. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-3b|1:3b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128b|128b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|230b]]; [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-117a|3:117a]].

    Here Rabbi El’azar draws on this midrashic teaching to indicate that Abraham is equivalent to the entire world and therefore constantly deserving of divine compassion.

[^215]: **יהיה (*Yihyeh*), *Will become*… thirty righteous humans…** The word יהיה (*yihyeh*), *will become*, is numerologically equivalent to thirty, implying that the world will always contain at least thirty righteous heroes.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:3: “*Abraham* יהיה‎ היו (*hayoh yihyeh*), *will surely become, a great and powerful nation*. Rabbi Tanḥum… said in the name of Rabbi Berekhiah, ‘He assured him that the world would never lack thirty righteous humans like Abraham.’ Rabbi Yudan and Rabbi Aḥa, in the name of Rabbi Alexandra deduced it from here: *Abraham* יהיה‎ היה (*hayoh yihyeh*), *will surely become:* י (*yod*) is ten, ה (*he*) five, י (*yod*) ten, and ה (*he*) five.”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 35:2; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.05 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^zh-26b|26b]] (*MhN*); Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:239, n. 164.

[^216]: ***Of the thirty he was most honored*…** The actual biblical verse does not include the word *most* (הכי \[*ha-khi*\]). Apparently, the author or a scribe substituted another phrase, several verses earlier, describing another of King David’s warriors, Avishai: (*Of the three*) *he was most honored* ([[II Samuel 23#^23-19|2 Samuel 23:19]]). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6b|1:6b]].

[^217]: **Benayahu son of Yehoyada…** A loyal follower of King David. See [[II Samuel 23#^23-20|2 Samuel 23:20–23]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6a|1:6a]]–b.

[^218]: **three others, on whom the world stands…** The three patriarchs: Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, who symbolize the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*. See BT *Ḥagigah* 12b: “Rabbi El’azar son of Shamu’a says, ‘\[The world stands\] upon a single pillar named Righteous One, as is said: *The righteous one is the foundation of the world.’”*

[^219]: ***A torrent bursts forth against strangers, forgotten of foot*…** In rabbinic literature this and the following verses are often applied to Sodom and Gomorrah. See *Mekhilta, Shirta* 2; *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 43; *Tosefta, Sotah* 3:12; BT *Sanhedrin* 109a; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 26:2; *Tanḥuma, Beshallaḥ 12*.

[^220]: **being refused food or drink** See BT *Sanhedrin* 109b.

[^221]: **there is written: *Her paths wander*** Referring to the *forbidden woman* (ibid., 3) who strays and leads men astray.

[^222]: **Even birds of heaven avoided entering** … See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25: “Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Korḥah said, ‘… They cut all \[the branches of\] the trees where fruit grew and above, so that there would be no benefit from them—even to the birds of heaven, as is said: *a path unknown to any vulture.’”*

[^223]: **Sodom and Gomorrah and all those cities** There were five in all. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:4.

[^224]: **why *if according to her cry*?…** Why the singular? See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25: “Rabbi Yehudah said, ‘They issued a proclamation in Sodom: Anyone who strengthens the hand of the poor or the strangers will be burnt by fire. Peletit daughter of Lot was married to one of the nobles of Sodom. She saw an afflicted poor man in the street of the city, and her soul grieved for him. What did she do? Every day when she went out to draw water, she put in her bucket all sorts of food from her home, and she fed that poor man. The people of Sodom said, ‘How does this poor man survive?’ Finally they discovered the matter—and brought her out to be burned. She said, ‘Lord of the world! Uphold my just cause against the people of Sodom!’ Her cry ascended before the Throne of Glory. The blessed Holy One said, “*I will go down and see* \[*if they have done altogether*\] *according to her cry that has come to Me*. If the people of Sodom have done according to the cry of this girl, I will overturn its foundations and its surface!”—as is said: *according to her cry*. It is not written: *according to their cry*, but rather: *according to her cry.’”*

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:6; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-21|Genesis 18:21]]. Cf. *Sefer ha-Yashar, Vayera*, 104–6; *Midrash Aggadah*, [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-20|Genesis 18:20]]; BT *Sanhedrin* 109b.

[^225]: **side beneath the hailstone** The side of stern judgment, whose arousal is outlined in the following cryptic lines. On the phrase קלא‎ תותי (*tutei qala*), “beneath the stone \[or: clod\],” see BT *Ta’anit* 4a.

[^226]: **thick cluster כתפא** (*Katpa*), “Shoulder.” In M *Pe’ah* 7:4 and *Tosefta, Pe’ah* 3:11, the Hebrew equivalent refers to the dense top of a cluster of grapes.

[^227]: **chasms of the immense abyss** The abode of demons.

[^228]: **becoming five-in-one** Constituting five aspects of stern judgment.

[^229]: **voices** Voices of accusation against evil.

[^230]: **voice ascending from below** Apparently the voice of a wronged human calling out for justice.

[^231]: **Decree of Judgment** The divine Attribute of Judgment. See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-20|Genesis 18:20]].

[^232]: **demanding justice for the sale of Joseph…** According to rabbinic sources, the selling of Joseph by his ten brothers was eventually punished through the martyrdom of ten rabbis during the Hadrianic persecution in the second century.

    See *Heikhalot Rabbati* 5:5 (in *Battei Midrashot*, 1:74): “The Attribute of Judgment stood before the blessed Holy One and said, ‘Master of the World! You wrote in Your Torah: *One who steals a man and sells him*… \[*will surely be put to death*\] ([[Torah 18 - Mishpatim#^21-16|Exodus 21:16]]). Now, the sons of Israel \[Jacob\] who stole their brother Joseph and sold him, what has happened to them?’ Immediately permission was granted to Samael to annihilate ten heroes in their place.”

    See *Midrash Mishlei* 1:13; *Elleh Ezkerah* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 2:64); *[[Zohar 11.05 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Eikhah#^zh-93b|ZḤ* 93b]] (*MhN, Eikhah*).

[^233]: ***In the evening she* באה (*ba’ah*), *comes*…** Describing the continual stream of maidens coming to King Ahasuerus in the contest to be crowned queen. The following verses describe Esther, so this verse is sometimes applied specifically to her and to the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-202b|1:202b]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-220b|3:220b]].

[^234]: **This is *ha-ba’ah, coming, to Me*, constantly** Reading the verb in the present tense rather than the past. The Attribute of Judgment constantly demands justice.

[^235]: ***End of all flesh*…** The Angel of Death, looming constantly. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|54a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58a|58a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|62b]].

[^236]: **silent, saying nothing, not pleading for mercy** Noah is criticized for not saving his generation in *Devarim Rabbah* 11:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-67b|1:67b]]–68a; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14b|3:14b–15a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-23a|23a]] (*MhN*).

[^237]: **beginning with fifty, until ten** Abraham opened by pleading with God to save Sodom if there were fifty innocent people there, and he continued until reaching the number ten. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-23|Genesis 18:23–32]].

[^238]: **Abraham said, ‘I don’t want to claim reward for my deeds’** He was too humble to demand that God save the residents of Sodom on account of his own merit. See above, page 141.

[^239]: ***I forgive, according to your word*** This verse actually appears in the story of the spies in Numbers, not in the context of the Golden Calf.

    See *Tanḥuma, Ki Tissa* 31; *Shemot Rabbah* 51:4; *Devarim Rabbah* 3:15; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 46, and David Luria, ad loc., n. 81; *Da’at Zeqenim* (*Ba’alei ha-Tosafot*) on [[Torah 37 - Sh'lach#^14-20|Numbers 14:20]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14b|3:14b]].

[^240]: **otherwise not** Abraham did not plead for the guilty themselves, as Moses had done.

[^241]: **Moses, Faithful Shepherd** This title is applied to Moses in *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ* 6; *Sifrei Zuta* 27; *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 24; *Ester Rabbah* 7:13; *Rut Rabbah, Petiḥta* 5. See *Tanḥuma, Shemot 7*.

[^242]: **beginning with fifty, beginning of knowing** See above, note 237. Here fifty symbolizes *Binah*, the highest *sefirah* accessible to human searching. She is linked with this number based on BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 21b: “Rav and Shemu’el both said, ‘Fifty gates of בינה (*binah*), understanding, were created in the world, all of which were given to Moses except for one, as is said: *You made him little less than God* ([[Psalms 008#^8-6|Psalms 8:6]]).’”

[^243]: **until ten, who is tithe, end of all rungs** Ten symbolizes *Shekhinah*, last of the ten *sefirot* and known as “tithe.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87b|1:87b]].

[^244]: **ten days between Rosh Hashanah and Yom Kippur** “The Ten Days of Returning \[to God\].”

[^245]: **returning** Turning back to God.

[^246]: ***as Lot was sitting at the gate of Sodom*…** The verse continues: *Lot saw and rose to greet them and bowed, face to the ground*.

[^247]: **Come and see what is written above: *YHVH left*…** In the preceding verse.

[^248]: **since one disappeared with *Shekhinah*, leaving these two** Originally, three angels had appeared to Abraham ([[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-2|Genesis 18:2]]). See BT *Bava Metsi’a* 86b; and *Bereshit Rabbah* 50:2: “One angel does not perform two missions, nor do two angels perform one mission, yet the verse reads: \[*The*\] *two* \[*angels came to Sodom*\]*!* However, Michael delivered his tidings \[of the birth of Isaac\] and departed, Gabriel was sent to overturn Sodom, and Rafael to deliver Lot, so *The two angels came*….”

[^249]: **Look how they punished his daughter!** See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25, cited above, note 224, and parallels listed there.

[^250]: **Because it is written: *Lot saw and ran to greet them*** Because of what he saw, he ran to greet them. Actually, [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-1|Genesis 19:1]] does not mention running but reads rather: *Lot saw and rose to greet them*.

[^251]: **Just as there he saw *Shekhinah*** Abraham saw *Shekhinah*, who is symbolized by *the opening of the tent* because She is the opening of the divine realm. See above, pages 119–20, 131–32.

[^252]: **אדני (*Adonai*), *my Lord*, spelled יוד‎ נון‎ דלת‎ אלף (*alef, dalet, nun, yod*)** The word is usually translated *my lords*, but can also be rendered *my Lord*, one of the names of *Shekhinah*.

    See BT *Shevu’ot* 35b; Rashi on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-3|Genesis 18:3]]; [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-18|19:18]]; Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-2|Genesis 19:2]], 18; *Minḥat Shai* on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-3|Genesis 18:3]]; [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-2|19:2]], 18; Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Yesodei ha-Torah* 6:9; above, page 125.

[^253]: ***Please turn aside*** The verse continues: *to your servant’s house and spend the night*.

[^254]: **so that the townspeople wouldn’t see** them… See *Bereshit Rabbah* 50:4: “*Please turn aside*…. Rabbi Huna said, ‘Come to me by a circuitous route, so that you will not be seen approaching me.’”

[^255]: **Torah is a tree of life** Torah is identified with the Tree of Life, based on the description of wisdom in [[Proverbs 03#^3-18|Proverbs 3:18]]: *She is a tree of life to those who grasp her*. See BT *Berakhot* 32b, 61b.

[^256]: **He watches over her constantly…** Over the land of Israel, symbolizing *Shekhinah*.

[^257]: ***From afar she brings her food*** *Shekhinah* draws the flow of emanation from the highest reaches of the *sefirot*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-50b|2:50b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-60a|3:60a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-90a|90a]].

[^258]: **all those beasts of the field** The forces (including the *ḥayyot*, “living creatures, animals, beasts”) beneath *Shekhinah*, who is known as “field” or Holy Apple Orchard.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|142b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-61b|2:61b]]. Cf. BT *Ta’anit* 29b; *Bereshit Rabbah* 65:22; Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 35–37.

[^259]: **Now, who can see *Shekhinah*?** See [[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^33-20|Exodus 33:20]]: *No human can see Me and live!* Cf. BT *Ketubbot* 111b, *Sotah* 14a.

[^260]: **‘אדני (*Adonai*), *my Lord,’* spelled יוד‎ נון‎ דלת‎ אלף (*alef, dalet, nun, yod*)…** The word is usually translated *my lords*, but can also be rendered *my Lord*, one of the names of *Shekhinah*. See above, note 252.

[^261]: **so that people wouldn’t discover them** If anyone saw them, it would seem as though they had just arrived, since the dust was still on their feet.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 50:4: “*Spend the night, and wash your feet*. Abraham first asked them to wash and then to stay, while Lot first asked them to spend the night and then to wash. However, Abraham was strict about the pollution of idolatry, while Lot was not. Some say: Even this Lot did properly, so that when they left, the dust would be seen on their feet, and \[the residents of Sodom\] would not ask, ‘Where did they spend the night?’”

[^262]: ***He urged them strongly*…** The verse continues: *so they turned aside to him and entered his house*.

[^263]: **a single messenger acts. Yet now we see two messengers. Why?…** See above, page 148; BT *Bava Metsi’a* 86b; and *Bereshit Rabbah* 50:2: “One angel does not perform two missions, nor do two angels perform one mission, yet the verse reads: \[*The*\] *two* \[*angels came to Sodom*\]! However \[of the three angels who originally appeared to Abraham\], Michael delivered his tidings \[of the birth of Isaac\] and departed, Gabriel was sent to overturn Sodom, and Rafael to deliver Lot, so *The two angels came*….”

[^264]: ***Behold, the day of YHVH is coming, cruel with wrath*** The verse continues: *and fury, to make the earth a desolation and annihilate its sinners from it*. Later in this chapter (verse 19) Sodom and Gomorrah are mentioned.

[^265]: **Lower Court of Justice** Symbolizing *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment, Justice”) and administers it to the world.

[^266]: ***Is coming*—as has been said: *coming to Me*** The verse in Genesis reads: *I will go down and see if they have done altogether according to her cry that has come to Me*. See above, page 146, where the verb הבאה (*ha-ba’ah*) is understood in the present tense: *coming* \[*to Me*\], referring to the Attribute of Judgment who constantly demands justice.

[^267]: **only after entering and obtaining authorization** See the description of Satan’s itinerary in BT *Bava Batra* 16a: “He descends and seduces, ascends and arouses wrath, obtains authorization, and seizes the soul.”

    See Rashi on BT *Shabbat* 89a, s.v. *ba hasatan; Zohar* 1:10b, 46b, 125a, 148a (*ST*), 152b; 2:33b, 268b.

[^268]: ***End of all flesh*…** The Angel of Death. See above, note 235.

[^269]: **Destroyer below** The Angel of Death.

[^270]: ***I will make a human more precious than pure gold*…** In Isaiah the meaning is: *rarer than pure gold*, since nearly everyone will be destroyed, but Rabbi Ḥiyya understands it differently.

[^271]: **these verses** From Isaiah.

[^272]: **those above and below** Angels and human beings.

[^273]: **Although this prophecy was proclaimed over Babylon** See [[Isaiah 13#^13-1|Isaiah 13:1]]: *The oracle concerning Babylon, envisioned by Isaiah son of Amoz*.

[^274]: **ויהוה (*Va-YHVH*), *And YHVH*…rung of Lower Court of Justice…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:2: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Wherever it is said: *And YHVH*, this implies: He and His Court.’” In Kabbalah this court symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment, Justice”) and pronounces the divine decree, so the phrase *And YHVH* encompasses “He \[*Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*\] and His Court \[*Shekhinah*\]*.”*

    See JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b; Rashi on [[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-29|Exodus 12:29]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64b|64b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|2:37b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-44b|44b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-149a|3:149a]]. The hermeneutical significance of *and* was championed by Rabbi Akiva. See BT *Yevamot* 68b; *Sanhedrin* 51b.

[^275]: **Executing judgment with compassion** To Rabbi Yitsḥak, the wording *And YHVH* implies that the punishment was tempered, since the divine name *YHVH* conveys compassion. See *Sifrei*, [[Torah 50 - Ki Tavo#^26-1|Deuteronomy 26]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15; 33:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-47b|2:47b]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142b|3:142b]] (*IR*).

[^276]: ***from YHVH out of heaven*** The verse reads: *And YHVH rained brimstone and fire on Sodom and Gomorrah, from YHVH out of heaven*.

[^277]: ***God remembered Abraham and sent Lot out*** The verse continues: *of the midst of the upheaval*

[^278]: **two entire nations issued from him** Ammon and Moab, descended from Lot and his daughters. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-30|Genesis 19:30–38]].

[^279]: **among his descendants King David and King Solomon** David and his son Solomon were descended from Ruth the Moabite and Boaz. See [[Ruth 4#^4-21|Ruth 4:21–22]].

[^280]: **When judgment looms over the world…** See BT *Bava Qamma* 60a–b: “Rabbi Yosef taught: ‘What is the meaning of the verse *None of you shall go out the door of his house until morning* ([[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-22|Exodus 12:22]])? Once permission has been granted to the Destroyer, he does not distinguish between righteous and wicked.’… Our Rabbis taught: ‘A plague in town? Keep your feet indoors.’ … Our Rabbis taught: ‘A plague in town? One should not walk in the middle of the road, because the Angel of Death walks there—for as soon as permission has been granted him, he strides brazenly.’”

    See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 11; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|1:63a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68a|68a–b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|69a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-101b|101b–102a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-113a|113a]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-182b|182b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-197b|197b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-204b|204b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|196a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-227a|227a]]; [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-54a|3:54a–b]].

[^281]: **Noah shut himself in the ark…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|1:63a]].

[^282]: ***None of you shall go out the door of his house until morning*** During the last of the ten plagues while the Destroyer is killing the Egyptian firstborn.

[^283]: **sinners in Hell are punished by water and fire** According to rabbinic literature, the yearlong punishment of the wicked in Hell is equally divided between fire and snow. See IT *Sanhedrin* 10:3, 29b; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 10:4; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|1:62b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68b|68b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-238b|238b]].

[^284]: **one from the side of water, one from the side of fire** Brimstone is apparently understood here as congealed sulfurous water. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-108a|1:108a]] (*ST*).

[^285]: **punishment of sinners in Hell lasts twelve months** See M *Eduyyot* 2:10: “\[Rabbi Akiva\] said, ‘The judgment of the generation of the Flood lasted twelve months… The judgment of the wicked in Hell lasts twelve months.’” See above, note 283.

    On the yearlong duration of the Flood, see [[Torah 02 - Noach#^7-11|Genesis 7:11]]; [[Torah 02 - Noach#^8-14|8:14]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 33:7; *Seder Olam Rabbah* 4; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68b|1:68b]].

[^286]: **Soon the blessed Holy One takes pity upon them** God feels compassion for the sinners who have already suffered in Hell for one year.

[^287]: **water pouring from above, boiling water from below like fire** The Flood consisted of freezing water raining down from the sky and boiling water surging up from the depths.

    See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Bereshit* 33; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 22; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|1:62a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68b|68b]].

[^288]: **This has already been discussed** According to M *Sanhedrin* 10:3, “The generation of the Flood has no share in the world to come; nor will they stand at the \[Last\] Judgment.” In *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 32, opinions differ as to whether or not this generation will be judged in the hereafter.

    See *Tosefta, Sanhedrin* 13:6; BT *Sanhedrin* 108a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68b|1:68b]]–69a; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:184, n. 44.

[^289]: **those of Sodom and Gomorrah will not arise** In M *Sanhedrin* 10:3, opinions differ as to whether the generation of Sodom will arise for judgment.

[^290]: ***Brimstone and salt, all its land burning*…** The verse reads: *Brimstone and salt, all its land burning, nothing sown, nothing sprouting, no vegetation springing up in it, like the overturning of Sodom and Gomorrah, Admah and Zeboiim, which YHVH overturned in His anger and His fury*.

[^291]: **Just as their land was destroyed forever and ever** Though according to [[Ezekiel 16#^16-55|Ezekiel 16:55]], Sodom will return to her former state. See *Shemot Rabbah* 15:21.

[^292]: **did not revive the soul of the needy…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:6; BT *Sanhedrin* 109b; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25; above, page 144.

[^293]: **charity, which is life** See [[Proverbs 12#^12-28|Proverbs 12:28]]: *In the path of charity is life*. Cf. 11:19; 21:21; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 34.

[^294]: **blocked roads and paths for inhabitants of the world** See *Mekhilta, Shirta* 2; *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 43; *Tosefta, Sotah* 3:12; BT *Sanhedrin* 109a; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 26:2; *Tanḥuma, Beshallaḥ* 12.

[^295]: **All will arise—will arise for judgment** Including the inhabitants of Sodom. See M *Sanhedrin* 10:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|1:69a]]; above, note 289.

[^296]: ***these to shame and everlasting contempt*** The verse reads: *Many of those who sleep in the dust of earth will awake, these to everlasting life, these to shame and everlasting contempt*.

[^297]: **Lot dwelled in them all…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:6; *Sekhel Tov*, ad loc.; above, page 141.

[^298]: **King of Sodom… welcomed him on account of Abraham** Since Abraham had defeated the confederacy of eastern kings who attacked the King of Sodom and his fellow vassals. See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]].

[^299]: ***His wife looked behind him*** The verse continues: *and she became a pillar of sat*. The phrase *behind him* is usually interpreted as “behind Lot.”

[^300]: **it means ‘behind *Shekhinah’*** See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-17|Genesis 19:17]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-224b|2:224b]]. In rabbinic literature the term *Shekhinah* is not overtly feminine, as in the *Zohar*.

[^301]: **Behind Lot, for the Destroyer was following him** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-197a|2:197a]].

[^302]: **He was the one who sent him away!** The two angels who visited Lot in Sodom told him to flee ([[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-12|Genesis 19:12–17]]). According to rabbinic tradition, one of these angels was Gabriel, who overturned the city. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 50:2; BT *Bava Metsi’a* 86b; above, pages 148–50.

[^303]: **as long as the Destroyer doesn’t see a person’s face…** See *Devarim Rabbah* 4:4; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|1:63a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68b|68b]].

[^304]: **the blessed Holy One apportioned all nations…** According to rabbinic tradition, Israel is ruled directly by God, while the other seventy nations of the world are governed by angels or heavenly princes appointed by Him.

    See [[Daniel 10#^10-20|Daniel 10:20]]; Septuagint, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; Jubilees 15:31–32; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-8|Genesis 11:8]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-19|Genesis 9:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 24; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|61a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84b|84b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33a|2:33a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-151b|151b]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-298b|3:298b]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:204–5, n. 91.

[^305]: **The blessed Holy One provides nourishment there first…** See BT *Ta’anit* 10a: “The land of Israel is watered by the blessed Holy One Himself, while the rest of the world is watered by a messenger…. The land of Israel drinks water directly from the rain, while the rest of the world drinks of the drippings.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84b|1:84b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-152b|2:152b]]; [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-209b|3:209b]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 46 - Eikev#^11-10|Deuteronomy 11:10]].

[^306]: ***In which*—in sanctity of the land…** The repetition of the phrase *in which* signifies the holiness of the land of Israel, in which dwells *Shekhinah*, the supernal land, culmination of the sefirotic realm of faith.

[^307]: ***like the garden of YHVH, like the land of Egypt*** Describing the plain of Jordan, including Sodom and Gomorrah.

[^308]: **it is unclear whether *the garden of YHVH*…** Is the verse identifying *the garden of YHVH* with the land of Egypt or with Sodom? Or is Sodom being compared with the Garden of Eden and with Egypt?

[^309]: ***like the garden of YHVH*—containing total satisfaction and delight** Like the Garden of Eden.

[^310]: **Just as *the garden of YHVH* needs no human watering** See [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-10|Genesis 2:10]]: *A river issues from Eden to water the garden*.

[^311]: **neither does Egypt** Which is watered by the Nile. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-268b|3:268b]].

[^312]: **Since it possessed all delights of the world…** Since Sodom was so fertile and delightful, the residents wanted to keep it all for themselves. See the sources cited above, note 294.

[^313]: **Whoever is lavish toward the poor…** See BT *Megillah* 28a: “Rabbi Neḥunya son of ha-Kanah was asked by his disciples, ‘How did you live so long?’ He replied, ‘I never gained honor through shaming another, I never climbed into bed accompanied by another’s curse, and I was generous with my money.’”

[^314]: ***wheels*** מסבות (*Mesibbot*), “Those which are round, circles.” NJV renders the opening clause: *He keeps turning events by His stratagems*.

[^315]: **spins revolutions** סבובין‎ מסבב (*Mesabbev sibbuvin*), playing on the philosophical expression הסיבות‎ מסבב (*mesabbev ha-sibbot*), “revolves the turn of events.”

    See Radak on [[Isaiah 63#^63-9|Isaiah 63:9]]; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 354; idem, *Mishkan ha-Edut*, 52a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-111b|2:111b]]; Gersonides on [[I Kings 02#^2-46|1 Kings 2:46]]; Ben Yehuda, *Dictionary*, s.v., *mesibbah, savav, sibbah, sibbuv;* Liebes, *Peraqim*, 328–29. Cf. Maimonides, *Guide of the Perplexed* 1:70, where God is described as: הגלגל‎ מסבב (*mesabbev ha-galgal*), “revolving the \[celestial\] sphere.”

[^316]: **cloaked dazzlers** טהירין‎ קומרין (*Qumrin tehirin*), denoting heavenly powers, angelic or demonic. In *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|1:83a]], the phrase refers to malevolent forces who block the ascent of an unworthy soul. See 1:251a (*Hash*).

    *Qumrin* derives from the Greek *qamara*, “arched cover.” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 31:11; M *Eruvin* 8:9–10; *Arukh*, s.v. *qmr*; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61b|1:61b]].

    *Tehirin* derives from the Aramaic root meaning “brightness, noon.” One class of demons is named טהרי (*tiharei*), “noonday demons.” See [[Psalms 091#^91-6|Psalms 91:6]] and Rashi, ad loc; *Targum*, [[Song of Songs 4#^4-6|Song of Songs 4:6]]; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 35 - Nasso#^6-24|Numbers 6:24]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-24|Deuteronomy 32:24]]. The Hebrew root טהר (*thr*), “pure,” lends this demonic name a euphemistic tone.

[^317]: **As they act, so He transforms them** According to human action, God transforms the heavenly powers from a benevolent nature to a malevolent one, or vice-versa.

[^318]: **in all that He commands them *on the face of the earth*** Through commanding the angels to convey blessing or punishment below.

[^319]: **spelled בתחבולתו (*be-taḥbulato*), *by His device*** The word בתחבולותיו (*be-taḥbulotav*), *by His devices*, is spelled without the י (*yod*) and can thus be read in the singular: בתחבולתו (*be-taḥbulato*), *by His device*.

[^320]: **kick-wheel** טיקלא (*Tiqla*), defined in *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 178, as “a potter’s wheel rotated by the feet, known \[in Castilian\] as *torno.” Tiqla* is a Zoharic neologism derived from the root תקל (*tql*), “weigh,” here perhaps linked with the Greek *kuklos*, “wheel.” The word has a wide range of meaning in the *Zohar*: “scale, potter’s wheel, hollow of the hand, fist, water clock (above, pages 82–83).”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-95b|2:95b]]–96b; David ben Judah he-Ḥasid, *Mar’ot ha-Ẓove’ot*, 53, 77; ibid., Matt, intro, 29; Liebes, *Peraqim*, 327–35.

[^321]: **he can fashion as he imagines—this way or that…** See [[Jeremiah 18#^18-2|Jeremiah 18:2–6]]; Maimonides, *Guide of the Perplexed* 2:13; and the *piyyut* for Yom Kippur: *ki hinneh ka-ḥomer*.

[^322]: **Lower Court of Justice, a potter’s wheel…** *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment, Justice”) and administers it to the world. She is also known as *Sod ha-Efshar* (“Secret of the Possible”), since through Her, creation is continually formed and transformed. See Matt, intro to *Mar’ot ha-Ẓove’ot*, by David ben Judah he-Ḥasid, 29.

[^323]: **If they act well, that kick-wheel spins them to the right…** The potter’s wheel, symbolizing *Shekhinah*, spins to the right (symbolizing *Ḥesed*) or to the left (*Din*), depending on human conduct. Here *tiqla* implies both “kick-wheel” and “scale” of judgment.

[^324]: **Ishmael issued from him, since Abraham was then uncircumcised…** Since Abraham had not yet entered the covenant of circumcision, his firstborn son, Ishmael, was imperfect. See above, page 135.

[^325]: **being perfected through his name, called אברהם (*Avraham*)…** אברם (*Avram*) was transformed into אברהם (*Avraham*) by the addition of the letter ה (*he*), symbolizing *Binah*, the Divine Mother. She is also known as Spirit, from which derives Water, symbolizing *Ḥesed*, the *sefirah* of Abraham. See *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:11; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|1:96a]]; *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72b|ZḤ* 72b]] (*ShS*).

[^326]: **Isaac issued from him: holy seed… fire from water** Isaac represents *Gevurah*, also symbolized by fire, which issues from *Ḥesed*, symbolized by water. The phrase “fire from water” derives from *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:12. See above, page 136.

[^327]: ***I planted you as a choice vine, entirely seed of truth*** The House of Jacob, addressed by Jeremiah (2:4), derives from holy seed, while Jacob himself symbolizes *Tif’eret*, known as Truth. See above, page 112.

[^328]: **Other Side** אחרא‎ סטרא (*Sitra aḥra*), the demonic realm.

[^329]: **two separate nations** Moab and Ammon. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-30|Genesis 19:30–38]].

[^330]: **side befitting them** The left side. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]].

[^331]: **revolves revolutions, rotates rotations** … God brings about events in the world purposefully. See above, note 315; Liebes, *Peraqim*, 299–300.

[^332]: **linked to the site befitting them** The incestuous union of Lot and his daughters engendered nations linked to the demonic realm.

[^333]: **They were generated from wine mysteriously…** Lot’s daughters made him drunk with wine, symbolizing the left side, so that Moab and Ammon would emerge from that side. Similarly, Noah *drank of the wine and became drunk, and exposed himself inside his tent*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73a|1:73a]]–b; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245a|2:245a]] (*Heikh*).

[^334]: **by them** By Lot’s daughters.

[^335]: ***Moab*** The verse reads: *The firstborn gave birth to a son and named him Moab. He is the father of Moab to this day*.

[^336]: ***The younger one also gave birth to a son and named him Ben-Ammi***. The verse continues: *He is the father of the children of Ammon to this day*.

[^337]: **Impudently she said… Decorously she said…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:11; BT *Nazir* 23b; Rashi on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-37|Genesis 19:37]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-188a|3:188a]].

[^338]: ***He was unaware of her lying down …or her rising*** The verse reads: *That night they made their father drink wine, and the firstborn one went in and lay with her father; he was unaware of her lying down or her rising*.

[^339]: **ובקו֗מה (*Uvqumah*), *or her rising*—spelled with a ו (*vav*), dotted above…** The word ובקו֗מה (*uvqumah*) is spelled with a second ו (*vav*), a letter of the divine name יהוה (*YHVH*). Furthermore, this letter is dotted. Both these Masoretic details invite midrashic interpretation and, according to Rabbi Yose, imply divine assistance. God approved and encouraged this apparently sinful act, since the Davidic Messiah is destined to descend from Ruth the Moabite.

    See [[Ruth 4#^4-21|Ruth 4:21–22]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-188a|1:188a]]–b; Liebes, “Sefer Tsaddiq Yesod Olam,” 87–88, n. 88.

    On this and other dotted letters in the Torah, see *Sifrei*, Numbers 69; BT *Nazir* 23a, *Bava Metsi’a* 87a; *Soferim* 6:3; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 34; B, 37; *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc.

[^340]: **Concerning the other, it is written: ובקמה** (*uvqumah*), ***or her rising*…** The verse reads: *They made their father drink wine that night also, and the younger one arose and lay with him; he was unaware of her lying down or her rising*. Here the word ובקמה (*uvqumah*), *or her rising*, is written without a second ו (*vav*), since the Messiah (“a share of the blessed Holy One”) did not issue from this second union.

    Actually, Na’amah the Ammonite, one of Solomon’s wives, was the mother of King Rehoboam in the Davidic line ([[I Kings 14#^14-21|1 Kings 14:21]]). According to one view, the Messiah is descended not from Na’amah and Solomon’s son Rehoboam but from a different son of David: Nathan. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-173b|3:173b]]; *Zohorei Ya’bets; NZ*, ad loc., n. 1, and on this passage, n. 3.

[^341]: ***She rose before one person could recognize another*** Describing Ruth’s action after sleeping with Boaz. The verse reads: *She lay at his feet until dawn. She rose before one person could recognize another*. The similar verbs in Genesis and Ruth link Lot’s firstborn daughter with her descendant, Ruth the Moabite.

[^342]: **That day she really rose—for Boaz united with her *to raise*…** That day Ruth rose in stature, because her marriage to Boaz enabled her to have a son who would carry on the name of her dead husband.

[^343]: **Come and see the humility of Abraham!** … See above, pages 140–41. According to *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25, at this point Abraham did plead for Lot. See David Luria, ad loc., n. 8.

[^344]: **deducted any of his merit** Deducted some of Abraham’s merit in exchange for having saved Lot.

[^345]: **against five mighty kings** Actually against four kings who were attacking five Canaanite vassal kings. See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]]; below, page 167.

[^346]: **Lot’s improper conduct** Lot is often portrayed negatively in rabbinic literature. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):17 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^13-10|Genesis 13:10]]); 51:6, 9; 52:2; BT *Nazir* 23a; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:240, n. 171; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-79a|1:79a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84a|84a]].

[^347]: **Abraham did not want the blessed Holy One—simply for his sake…** Abraham did not want God to forgive anything Lot had done, simply for Abraham’s sake.

[^348]: **All his journeyings were toward the side of the South…** South symbolizes the warmth of *Ḥesed* (“Love”), Abraham’s spiritual domain.

    See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-118a|118a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^349]: **he acted in wisdom, cleaving to the South** See BT *Bava Batra* 25b: “Rabbi Yitsḥak said, ‘One who desires to become wise should turn to the south \[when praying\]; one who desires to become rich should turn to the north.’”

[^350]: ***Abraham said of his wife Sarah, “She is my sister”*** Visiting the royal city of Gerar, Abraham pretends that Sarah is his sister, so that King Abimelech will not kill him and steal his wife.

[^351]: **A person should not rely on a miracle** See BT *Pesaḥim* 64b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|1:230a]]–b.

[^352]: **miracles do not happen every moment** See BT *Megillah* 7b: “\[Rabbi Zeira said,\] ‘Miracles do not happen every moment.’” Cf. *Pesaḥim* 50b.

[^353]: **If a person puts himself in obvious danger…** See BT *Shabbat* 32a: “Rabbi Yannai… said, ‘A person should never stand in a place of danger, saying that a miracle will be performed for him; perhaps it will not. And if a miracle is performed for him, it is deducted from his merits.’ Rabbi Ḥanin said, ‘Which verse demonstrates this? *I am diminished by all the kindness and faithfulness* \[*You have shown Your servant*\]*.’”*

    The verb in Genesis, קטנתי (*qatonti*), is usually rendered: *I am unworthy*, but Rabbi Ḥanin reads it hyperliterally: *I am diminished*.

[^354]: **Since Abraham had already gone up from Egypt and been saved once** See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-10|Genesis 12:10–20]]; above, pages 19–29.

[^355]: **he saw *Shekhinah* abiding constantly with Sarah…** So he felt sure that She would protect both of them. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 60:16; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|1:50a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82a|82a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140b|140b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-52a|3:52a]].

[^356]: ***She is my sister… “You are my sister”*** *Shekhinah* is known as Lower Wisdom, deriving from the *sefirah* of *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”). The verse in Proverbs is applied to Her, and similarly, Abraham called Her *sister*. See Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 20; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-81b|1:81b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140b|140b]].

[^357]: **appointed messenger assigned to them…** The name *Elohim* is associated with the divine attribute of Judgment (*Sifrei*, [[Torah 50 - Ki Tavo#^26-1|Deuteronomy 26]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15; 33:3) and can refer to angelic messengers assigned to carry out acts of judgment. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-96a|2:96a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8a|3:8a]], [[Zohar 8.06 - Parashat Be-Huqqotai#^z3-113a|113a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-200a|200a]].

[^358]: ***You are about to die because of the woman you have taken*** The verse continues: *for she is a wedded wife!*

[^359]: **because of *Shekhinah*—who accompanied Sarah—he said *She is my sister*** See above, page 162.

[^360]: **Since Abraham constitutes the right side…** Abraham symbolizes *Ḥesed*, on the right side of the sefirotic tree, below *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”). Since emanation flows from *Ḥesed* to *Shekhinah*, Abraham was intimate with Her.

[^361]: ***My sister, my love, my dove, my perfect one!*** The beloved female in Song of Songs is identified with *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140b|1:140b]].

[^362]: **At last** After God ordered Abimelech to return Sarah to Abraham.

[^363]: **Was it so?** Sarah was not the daughter of Terah, Abraham’s father, but rather—according to rabbinic tradition—the daughter of Haran, Abraham’s brother.

[^364]: **Why *ve-gam?* To amplify…** In rabbinic hermeneutics the word גם (*gam*), “also, besides,” amplifies the literal meaning of a biblical word or phrase. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:14; JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b.

[^365]: ***my sister, my father’s daughter*, daughter of supernal Wisdom** *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”), is considered His daughter and is called “Lower Wisdom.” Since *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham, also derives from *Ḥokhmah*, Abraham and *Shekhinah* are siblings.

[^366]: ***though not my mother’s daughter*** *Shekhinah*, “Lower Wisdom,” is more closely related to *Ḥokhmah* than to *Binah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-100b|3:100b]].

[^367]: **realm of origin of all, concealed, supreme** *Ḥokhmah*, the primordial point of emanation.

[^368]: ***his right hand embracing me*** According to Kabbalah, these words are spoken by *Shekhinah*, describing how She is embraced by *Ḥesed*, the divine right hand.

[^369]: **faith** *Shekhinah*, who includes all the *sefirot*, the aspects of divine reality.

[^370]: **foreign rungs** The demonic realm. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-242b|2:242b]].

[^371]: ***for his virgin sister, spoken of the priest*…** A priest is forbidden to defile himself by contact with the dead, but an exception is made for his close relatives, including *his virgin sister*. Here the priest symbolizes *Ḥesed*, Abraham’s *sefirah*.

[^372]: ***YHVH your God you shall hold in awe, and Him you shall serve*…** The verse reads: *Him you shall serve*, not *and Him you shall serve*.

[^373]: **This verse has been established** See BT *Pesaḥim* 22b: “Rabbi Akiva came and taught: ‘יהוה‎ את (*Et YHVH*), *YHVH, your God you shall hold in awe*—including scholars.’” The hermeneutical principle is explained below, note 375.

[^374]: **It is not written: ליהוה (*La-YHVH*) *your God*…** The preposition ל (*la*) does not appear here, but rather the accusative particle את (*et*).

[^375]: **What is את (*Et*)? First rung…** Grammatically, the accusative particle את (*et*) has no ascertainable independent sense, but Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when *et* appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT *Pesaḥim* 22b; *Ḥagigah* 12a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|1:247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

    In the *Zohar* the word את (*et*) symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who comprises the entire alphabet of divine speech, from א (*alef*) to ת (*tav*). See the Christian parallel in [[Revelation 01#^1-8|Revelation 1:8]]: “I am *alpha* and *omega*.”

    *Shekhinah*, last of the ten *sefirot*, is the first one encountered by the spiritual seeker. She inspires awe.

[^376]: **this being the Court of Justice** Shekhinah derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment, Justice”) and administers it to the world.

[^377]: **ואותו (*Ve-Oto*), *And Him, you shall serve*—higher rung…** *Yesod*, the masculine partner of *Shekhinah*.

[^378]: **What is ואותו (*ve-oto*)? Site of the holy covenant, eternal אות (*ot*), sign** *Yesod*, the divine phallus, is symbolized by circumcision, the covenantal sign. See *Shemot Rabbah* 1:20; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-94a|1:94a]]; [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-184a|3:184a]].

[^379]: **service does not pertain to *Et*… service focuses above…** If *Shekhinah* becomes the focus of worship, She is cut off from Her partner. By focusing on *Yesod*, the human worshiper stimulates the union of the divine couple.

[^380]: **where cleaving cleaves, the torso abiding in the center** *Tif’eret*, central *sefirah* and trunk of the divine body.

[^381]: ***By His name*… seventh of rungs** *Shekhinah*, seventh of the lower *sefirot*, conveys the divine essence and is thus known as *His name*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-204b|1:204b]].

[^382]: **Your mnemonic: *David their king, whom I will raise*…** King David symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). His restoration reflects Her reunion with the higher *sefirot*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72b|1:72b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-240a|2:240a]].

[^383]: **faith** *Shekhinah*.

[^384]: ***I did this with an innocent heart and clean hands*** “When I took Sarah, I thought she was Abraham’s sister, not his wife.”

[^385]: **while *clean hands* are not mentioned** See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 25: “*God said to him in a dream, ‘Yes, I know that you did this with an innocent heart.’* The blessed Holy One said, ‘About one you spoke truly; about one you lied! Innocence of heart is here, but not cleanness of hands.’ Know that it is so: *Clean hands* are not mentioned here, only *an innocent heart.”*

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 52:6: “This indicates there was fondling by hand.” See Theodor, ad loc.; Rashi on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^20-6|Genesis 20:6]].

[^386]: ***He guards the feet of… His faithful ones*…** The full verse reads: *He guards the feet of His faithful ones, but the wicked are silenced in darkness, for not by might does one prevail*.

[^387]: **spelled חסידו (*ḥasido*), *His faithful one*** The abbreviated spelling (without a suffixal י \[*yod*\]) enables Rabbi Yehudah to read the word in the singular. See BT *Yoma* 38b; *Midrash Shemu’el* 5:16; *Aggadat Bereshit* 46:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-189a|1:189a]].

[^388]: **As to the expression *the feet of*, this means ‘his wife’** See [[II Samuel 11#^11-8|2 Samuel 11:8]]: *Go down to your house and bathe your feet*. See ibid., 11; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-8b|1:8b]]; cf. [[Ruth 3#^3-7|Ruth 3:7]].

    Similarly, God’s command to Moses, *Remove your sandals from your feet* ([[Torah 13 - Shemot#^3-5|Exodus 3:5]]), is interpreted to mean: “Refrain from intercourse with your wife.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-222a|2:222a]]; [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|3:148a]], [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-180a|180a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.11 - Parashat Ki Tetse#^zh-59b|59b]].

[^389]: **on the night he pursued them** On the night Abraham pursued them.
