[^390]: ***silenced by darkness*—night linked with darkness…** The word בחשך (*ba-ḥoshekh*) can be rendered: *in darkness* or *by darkness*. Here night symbolizes *Shekhinah*, while darkness symbolizes Her source of power, *Gevurah*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|1:133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-136a|136a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142a|142a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-168b|168b]].

[^391]: ***He divided himself against them by night, he and his servants*…** The simple meaning is that Abraham divided or deployed his forces against the kings.

[^392]: ***He divided himself against them by night*—the blessed Holy One…** The subject is no longer Abraham but God, who by withdrawing compassion enabled unmitigated judgment to attack. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-92b|1:92b]].

[^393]: **The verse should read: *And they smote them*** Since the subject is Abraham and his forces.

[^394]: **he and Eliezer were all alone** According to [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-14|Genesis 14:14]], Abraham mustered 318 men to fight the kings. Midrashic tradition interprets this number as signifying Abraham’s servant, Eliezer. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):2: “*Three hundred and eighteen*. Rabbi Shim’on son of Lakish said, ‘It was Eliezer alone, the numerical value of אליעזר (*Eli’ezer*) being 318.’”

[^395]: **Where danger prevails, one should not rely on a miracle** See BT *Qiddushin* 39b: “Wherever injury is likely, we do not rely on a miracle.” Cf. *Shabbat* 32a; above, page 162.

[^396]: **Abraham pursued five kings to wage war!** Actually, Abraham pursued four kings who were attacking five Canaanite vassal kings. See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]]; above, page 161.

[^397]: **he took money to ransom him** See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Lekh Lekha* 16; *Aggadat Bereshit* 13:3.

[^398]: **he saw *Shekhinah* shining in front of him** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]]).

[^399]: **Isaac joined him, and they fell before him… *silenced in darkness*…** Abraham, who symbolizes *Ḥesed*, was empowered by *Gevurah*, symbolized by Isaac and *darkness*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|1:133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142a|142a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-168b|168b]].

[^400]: **might always manifests on the right** See [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^15-6|Exodus 15:6]]: *Your right hand, O YHVH, majestic in might; Your right hand, O YHVH, shatters the enemy*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|1:86b]].

[^401]: **if it had not been encompassed by the left side** If *Ḥesed* on the right (symbolized by Abraham) had not been joined by *Gevurah* on the left (symbolized by Isaac).

[^402]: ***feet of His faithful one*—his wife** See above, note 388.

[^403]: ***YHVH plagued Pharaoh …at the word of*…** The full verse reads: *YHVH plagued Pharaoh and his household with great plagues because of Sarai, Abram’s wife*. Drawing on midrash, Rabbi Shim’on reads the phrase שרי‎ דבר‎ על (*al devar*), *because of Sarai* hyperliterally: *at the word of Sarai*. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):2: “דבר‎ על (*Al devar*), *At the word of, Sarai*… Rabbi Levi said, ‘That whole night an angel was standing with a whip in his hand. When she said, “Strike!” he struck; when she said, “Stop!” he stopped.’”

[^404]: **Site of the bundle in which souls are bound** See [[I Samuel 25#^25-29|1 Samuel 25:29]]: *The soul of my lord will be bound in the bundle of life*. Here the phrase describes *Shekhinah*, destination of the soul. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65b|1:65b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-224b|224b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 60–61 (75).

[^405]: **This is *the angel of YHVH*** One of the many names of *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|1:61a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-120b|120b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-166a|166a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187a|3:187a]]. Cf. *Mekhilta, Shirta* 3.

[^406]: ***And Satan*… evil impulse…** See BT *Bava Batra* 16a: “Resh Lakish said, ‘Satan, the evil impulse, and the Angel of Death are one and the same.’”

[^407]: **when Nebuchadnezzar cast him into the fire…** According to BT *Sanhedrin* 93a, King Nebuchadnezzar cast Joshua the high priest into the fire along with the false prophets Ahab son of Kolaiah and Zedekiah son of Maaseiah. The two false prophets burned to death, while Joshua emerged with only his clothes singed. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 33, and David Luria, ad loc., n. 95.

[^408]: **this one** Satan.

[^409]: **accusing only in time of danger** See *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^23-10|Deuteronomy 23:10]]: “Satan accuses only in time of danger.” Cf. *Bereshit Rabbah* 91:9; JT *Shabbat* 2:6, 5b.

[^410]: ***Some are swept away by injustice*** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:8; BT *Ḥagigah* 4b–5a; *Zohar* [[#^z1-119a|1:119a]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|2:196a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-249a|249a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-54b|3:54b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-59b|59b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-283b|283b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-291b|291b]] (*IZ*); *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-77c|ZḤ* 77c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^411]: **Either they should all be saved, or they should all be burned!** Joshua and both false prophets.

[^412]: **once the Destroyer is empowered… the innocent is not spared…** See BT *Bava Qamma* 60a–b: “Rabbi Yosef taught: ‘What is the meaning of the verse *None of you shall go out the door of his house until morning* ([[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-22|Exodus 12:22]])? Once permission has been granted to the Destroyer, he does not distinguish between righteous and wicked.’ … Our Rabbis taught: ‘A plague in town? Keep your feet indoors.’… Our Rabbis taught: ‘A plague in town? One should not walk in the middle of the road, because the Angel of Death walks there—for as soon as permission has been granted him, he strides brazenly.’”

    See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 11; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|1:63a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68a|68a–b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|69a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-102a|102a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-107b|107b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-182b|182b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-197b|197b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-204b|204b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|196a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-227a|227a]]; [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-54a|3:54a–b]].

[^413]: **drowning the others** The Egyptians.

[^414]: **miracle here, and judgment here—simultaneously!** See above, page 143.

[^415]: **That’s what was difficult for Him!** In rabbinic literature the supernatural feat of splitting the Red Sea serves as a paradigm of difficulty. Heavenly matchmaking of human couples and the nourishing of humanity are “as difficult as splitting the Red Sea.” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:4; 97(98):3 (on [[Torah 12 - Vayechi#^48-16|Genesis 48:16]]); BT *Pesaḥim* 118a, *Sotah* 2a.

[^416]: **above, everything is enacted completely… not in half** See BT *Yoma* 69b: “They do not grant half in heaven.” Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-21a|3:21a]].

[^417]: **until their guilt is complete…** See BT *Sotah* 8b–9a: “Rabbi \[Judah the Prince\] used to say, ‘How do we know that according to the measure with which one measures, it is measured out to him? As is said: *By exact measure, by exiling her You strive with her.’*… Rav Hamnuna said: ‘The blessed Holy One does not punish a person until his peck is full.’” Cf. Rashi on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^15-16|Genesis 15:16]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61b|1:61b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-121b|121b]].

    The verse from Genesis explains why Abraham’s descendants can inherit the Promised Land only in the distant future.

[^418]: **he accused Joshua, demanding that he be burned along with them** Satan demanded that all three be burned together since half-miracles are so difficult.

[^419]: ***angel of YHVH*** *Shekhinah.* See above, note 405.

[^420]: **Now,** if **you** say, ***‘YHVH said to Satan, “YHVH rebuke you, Satan!”’*** Obviously the subject is *YHVH*, not *the angel of YHVH*.

[^421]: **Sometimes, *the angel of YHVH;* sometimes, *YHVH*** Both names can refer to *Shekhinah*. On *YHVH* as a name of *Shekhinah*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-215a|2:215a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-9b|3:9b]], [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-166a|166a]].

[^422]: **He said to him *YHVH rebuke you, Satan*!…** *Shekhinah* (*the angel of YHVH*) called upon *YHVH* to rebuke Satan; She did not rebuke him Herself.

[^423]: **day that judgment appears in the world** Rosh Hashanah, when the world is judged. The Torah reading for the first day of Rosh Hashanah opens with [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-1|Genesis 21:1]]: *YHVH attended to Sarah*, the same verse that stimulates Rabbi Ḥiyya’s discourse (above, page 168). According to rabbinic tradition, Sarah conceived on Rosh Hashanah. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 73:1; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 17; BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 10b–11a.

[^424]: ***The elders of that city shall take a heifer*…** The context is an unsolved murder, which is atoned for by killing a heifer and making a public declaration.

[^425]: **break its neck with a hatchet** See M *Sotah* 9:5: “They break its neck with a hatchet from behind.” Normally, ritual slaughter is performed at the altar by cutting the front of the neck with a knife.

[^426]: **lured after this one!** After Satan.

[^427]: **So, *The land will not be purged*…** The murder can be atoned for only by the death of the murderer, who deprived the Angel of Death of the victim’s soul.

[^428]: **he stands poised to seduce the world…** The subject is Satan.

[^429]: **so they offer this calf…** God compassionately provides Israel with a stratagem to appease the satanic Angel of Death. The theme of assuaging demonic powers by granting them their share appears frequently in the *Zohar*. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 46: “They gave him \[Satan\] a bribe on Yom Kippur \[i.e., the scapegoat\] so that he would not nullify Israel’s sacrifice.”

    According to [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^16-1|Leviticus 16]], a scapegoat bearing the sins of Israel is offered on Yom Kippur to the wilderness demon Azazel. Similarly, in the Babylonian Akitu ritual a goat, substituted for a human being, is offered to Ereshkigal, goddess of the Abyss.

    See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^16-8|Leviticus 16:8]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-11a|1:11a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64a|64a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65a|65a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-174b|174b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-190a|190a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-210b|210b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-154b|2:154b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-237b|237b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-266b|266b]]; [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-63a|3:63a]] (*Piq*), [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-102a|102a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-202b|202b–203a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-258b|258b]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-87b|ZḤ* 87b]]–c (*MhN, Rut*); Moses de León, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 49; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:890–95.

[^430]: **Ox, cow, calf, heifer—all manifesting in supernal mystery…** In Ezekiel’s vision of the heavenly throne, the creatures carrying the throne *had the face of an ox on the left* ([[Ezekiel 01#^1-10|Ezekiel 1:10]]). In Kabbalah the ox symbolizes the left side, origin of the demonic, so it is appropriate that a heifer is offered here.

[^431]: ***Our hands did not spill this blood*…** The full verse reads: *They* \[*the city’s elders*\] *shall declare: “Our hands did not spill this blood, our eyes did not see.”* See M *Sotah* 9:6; and BT *Sotah* 46b: “Would it enter our minds that \[the members of\] a Court of Justice spill blood? Rather, ‘\[The victim\] did not come to us and leave our presence without food. We did not see him and let him go unescorted.’”

[^432]: **sound blended of fire, water, and air, becoming one** The sound of the shofar, generated by warm, moist breath, comprises three of the four elements. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-81b|2:81b]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-184b|184b]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-38b|3:38b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-99b|99b]] (*RM*), [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-261a|261a]].

[^433]: **site of the Throne of Judgment** *Shekhinah*, who derives from the quality of *Din* (“Judgment”) and conveys it to the world.

[^434]: **voice of Jacob** Symbolizing *Tif’eret*. See [[Torah 06 - Toldot#^27-22|Genesis 27:22]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-18a|3:18a]]–b, [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-100a|100a]].

[^435]: **so too above a shofar is aroused** Symbolizing *Binah*, source of the seven lower *sefirot*.

[^436]: **that sound blended of fire, water, and air harmonizes** The three elements symbolize the triad of *sefirot* issuing from *Binah: Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*.

[^437]: **Then that accuser is confounded** See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 16a–b: “Why do we sound a plain blast and a tremolo blast while sitting and then again while standing? In order to confound Satan.”

    See Rabbeinu Ḥananel on BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 35a (cited in *NZ*, 3:99b, n. 8); *Arukh*, s.v., *arev; Tosafot*, BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 16b, s.v., *kedei le-arbev; Zohar* 1:152a (*ST*); 2:184a–b, 196b, 237b–238a; 3:99b; *TZ* 21, 51b.

[^438]: **Rather, compassion joins judgment** … See *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15.

[^439]: **בכסא (*ba-kese*), *at the covering*** This rare word, spelled בכסה (*ba-keseh*) in the Masoretic text, is usually rendered *on the full moon*, but here Rabbi Shim’on interprets it according to the common root כסה (*ksh*), “to cover, conceal.” See *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc.; [[Proverbs 07#^7-20|Proverbs 7:20]].

[^440]: **moon is concealed…** See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 8a–b: “*Blow the shofar at the new moon, at the covering for our feast day*. Which is the feast on which the moon is covered? You must say it is Rosh Hashanah.” Cf. *Vayiqra Rabbah* 29:6.

    Here the moon symbolizes *Shekhinah*, whose light is concealed by the demonic powers. Alternatively, She conceals Herself from the demonic powers. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-131a|1:131a]]; [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-184a|2:184a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-100b|3:100b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231b|231b]], 275a. Correspondingly, when *Shekhinah* is weakened, the power of the demonic grows dominant. See 1:19b, 70b, 146a.

[^441]: **so he cannot approach** So Satan cannot approach *Shekhinah* to imbibe Her power or defile Her. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|1:138b]]; *ZḤ* 20C (*MhN*).

[^442]: **like someone roused from sleep** The expression appears in *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-114a|2:114a]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-136b|3:136b]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-293b|293b]] (*IZ*).

[^443]: **On Yom Kippur one must pacify and appease him with that goat…** See above, note 429.

[^444]: **arousal of compassion ascending from below** Through the sounding of the shofar.

[^445]: **makes Himself available throughout those ten days…** See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 18a; *Tanḥuma, Ha’azinu* **4.**

[^446]: **deferring them until Yom Kippur** When forgiveness is actually granted.

[^447]: **All depends on action and words, as we have established** The act of sounding the shofar and the words of prayer. Human words and action stimulate the powers above.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|3:92a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]], [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-120b|120b]].

[^448]: **“attending” for the female, “remembering” for the male** Actually, the verb *remember* also appears for the female, as in [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-22|Genesis 30:22]]: *God remembered Rachel*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115b|1:115b]] (*MhN*). Here, however, “the female” refers to the divine subject, not the human object, as indicated below. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-159b|1:159b]].

[^449]: ***I will surely return to you when life is due*** The verse continues: *and behold, your wife Sarah will have a son!*

[^450]: **The anonymous wording—*He said … implies*…** The anonymous subject is *Shekhinah*, the divine female, referred to here by the masculine pronoun. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-102b|1:102b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138a|138a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|142b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-60b|2:60b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-125b|125b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-131a|131a]].

[^451]: **Fruit of the handiwork… souls of the righteous** Souls are generated by the union of the divine couple, the blessed Holy One and *Shekhinah*. This fruit originates in the stream of emanation issuing from *Ḥokhmah* (symbolized by Eden).

    On souls as fruit, see *Bahir* 14 (22); Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 489, 504; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19a|19a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|33a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b–60a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85b|85b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 51; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 56 (69). Cf. Ibn Ezra on [[Psalms 001#^1-3|Psalms 1:3]].

[^452]: **flux of destiny** מזלא (*Mazzala*), “Constellation, planet, planetary influence, destiny, guardian angel.” In the *Zohar* it is associated with the root נזל (*nzl*), “to flow,” and often refers to the flow of emanation from the world of the male, which comprises *Binah* through *Yesod*, the divine phallus. *Yesod* conveys this flow and waters *the garden* of *Shekhinah*, engendering new souls.

[^453]: **children depend on this flux of destiny, not on any other site** See BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 28a: “Rava said, ‘Life, children, and sustenance do not depend on merit but on *mazzala*, destiny.’”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-43b|1:43b]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-137a|137a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-156b|156b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-159b|159b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|160b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-181a|181a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-207b|207b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-6a|2:6a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252b|252b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25b|3:25b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-134b|134b]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-289a|289a]] (*IZ*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|292b]] (*IZ*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-295b|295b]] (*IZ*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 193; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 65 (82); Gikatilla, *Sha’arei Orah*, 12a, 37a–b, 74a, 95a.

[^454]: **So it is written: *And YHVH attended… YHVH did*…** Whereas *YHVH* signifies *Tif’eret*, the conjunction *And* signifies *Shekhinah*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:2: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Wherever it is said: *And YHVH*, this implies: He and His Court.’” In Kabbalah this court symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment”) and pronounces the divine decree, so the phrase *And YHVH* encompasses “He \[*Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*\] and His Court \[*Shekhinah*\].”

    See JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b; Rashi on [[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-29|Exodus 12:29]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64b|64b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-105a|105a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-107b|107b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|2:37b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-44b|44b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-149a|3:149a]]. The hermeneutical significance of *and* was championed by Rabbi Akiva. See BT *Yevamot* 68b; *Sanhedrin* 51b.

    Here the point is that *Shekhinah* (acting in conjunction with *Tif’eret*) attended to Sarah, while the matriarch’s actual conception was brought about by *Tif’eret* Himself, conveying the flux.

[^455]: **one who attains children in this world thereby gains entrance…** See BT *Bava Batra* 116a: “Rabbi Yoḥanan said in the name of Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai, ‘Whoever does not leave a son to succeed him incurs the full wrath of the blessed Holy One.’”

    Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-48a|48a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-90a|90a]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-186b|186b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-228b|228b]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-89b|ZḤ* 89b]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^456]: **Who is *heritage of YHVH?* Land of the Living** *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65b|1:65b]]–66a, [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95b|95b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-124b|124b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-143b|143b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 62 (77).

[^457]: **land of Israel—Land of the Living** See *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 34: “The land of Israel is called ‘living,’ as is said: *I will set beauty in the land of the living* ([[Ezekiel 26#^26-20|Ezekiel 26:20]]).” Cf. *Targum Yonatan*, ad loc.; BT *Ketubbot* 111a. In Kabbalah the land of Israel symbolizes *Shekhinah*.

[^458]: **Tree of Life** Symbolizing *Tif’eret*.

[^459]: ***From Me your fruit appears*** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85b|1:85b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]].

[^460]: **Masters of judgment** Angels blocking the soul’s path.

[^461]: **guarantors in this world…** His children.

[^462]: **Open your mouth** See BT *Berakhot* 22a, *Eruvin* 54a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-4a|2:4a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-79a|3:79a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-105b|105b]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-85b|ZḤ* 85b]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^463]: **whenever people engage in words of Torah, *Shekhinah*** comes… See M *Avot* 3:2: “Rabbi Ḥananya son of Teradyon said, ‘… If two are sitting engaged in words of Torah, *Shekhinah* dwells between them.’”

    On the importance of engaging in Torah while on a journey, see [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-7|Deuteronomy 6:7]]; BT *Eruvin* 54a; *Ta’anit* 10b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58b|58b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|69b–70a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87a|87a]].

[^464]: **As long as the wife is concealed *in the recesses of your house*…** See *Tanḥuma, Vayishlaḥ* 6; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 8:9; *Midrash Tehillim* 128:3. Cf. *Vayiqra Rabbah* 20:11; BT *Yoma* 47a.

[^465]: **Just as a vine can be planted only… cannot be grafted…** See JT *Kil’ayim* 1:7, 27b: “\[Rabbi Levi said,\] *‘Your wife like a fruitful vine in the recesses of your house; your children like olive shoots around your table*. Just as olives cannot be grafted, so *your children* will include no unworthy offspring.’”

    Here Rabbi Yose transplants the simile from the end of the verse to the beginning.

    See BT *Sotah* 43b; *Midrash Shemu’el* 16; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.026a|1:26a]] (*TZ*), 239b; 3:125b, 247a (*RM*); *TZ 15*, 30b, 69, 111a; Löw, *Die Flora der Juden*, 1:166.

[^466]: **Just as leaves of olive trees do not shed all year long…** See BT *Menaḥot* 53b: “Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Levi said, ‘Why is Israel compared to an olive tree? To inform you that just as leaves of an olive tree do not shed either in summer or in winter, so Israel will never pass away either in this world or in the world that is coming.’”

[^467]: **The verse should read: כן‎ הנה (*Hinneh khen*), *Behold, so*** Why the apparently superfluous כי (*ki*), *surely?*

[^468]: **to amplify the meaning** לאסגאה (*Le-asga’ah*), “To increase,” a Zoharic rendering of the rabbinic Hebrew לרבות (*le-rabbot*), “to increase”—that is, to include, amplify, or widen the scope of meaning.

[^469]: **as long as *Shekhinah* was concealed in Her site fittingly** In the Temple, waiting for Her Husband, *Tif’eret*. See above, page 39; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-170b|2:170b]]–171a; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimtnon*, 359.

    The word כי (*ki*), *surely*, alludes to *Shekhinah* since its two letters form the initials of Her name ישראל‎ כנסת (*Keneset Yisra’el*), “Assembly of Israel.”

[^470]: ***Around your table*—eating, drinking, bringing offerings…** The altar in the Temple serves as God’s table.

[^471]: **Through them, those above and below are blessed** Israel’s worship ensures the flow of blessing through all the worlds.

[^472]: **Israel were banished from their father’s table** See BT *Berakhot* 3a: “Woe to the children banished from their father’s table!”

[^473]: **holy ones of the Most High** The phrase derives from [[Daniel 07#^7-18|Daniel 7:18]], where it refers to Israel.

[^474]: **punishment of Torah** The punishment for abandoning and violating Torah.

[^475]: **when the priests brought sacrifices and burnt offerings; this is *in joy*** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8a|3:8a]], [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-39a|39a]]; Rashi on BT *Yoma* 14a, s.v. *mi la mitterid*; Ta-Shma, *Ha-Nigleh she-ba-Nistar*, 105, n. 3.

[^476]: ***And gladness of heart*—the Levites** Who sang joyously in the Temple. See BT *Arakhin* 11a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8a|3:8a]].

[^477]: ***For the abundance of everything*—Israel, in between them…** Israel symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who mediates between—and draws from—the *sefirot* of *Ḥesed* (on the right) and of *Gevurah* (on the left), symbolized respectively by priests and Levites.

[^478]: ***You have magnified the nation*—Israel, over whom hovers the faith…** Israel’s grandeur is attested by the protection of *Shekhinah*, the realm of faith. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-236b|1:236b]].

[^479]: **rung of the supernal head, to which Abraham cleaved…** *Ḥesed* (“Love”), first of the seven lower *sefirot*, also known as *Gedullah* (“Greatness”). Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham attained this rung.

    See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]]; [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^480]: **when they ascend to cleave to You** Ascending to Jerusalem for the pilgrimage festivals.

[^481]: ***As in joy at the harvest*—Assembly of Israel…** The harvest festival of *Shavu’ot* commemorates the Revelation at Mount Sinai, when ישראל‎ כנסת (*Keneset Yisra’el*), “the Assembly of Israel,” was wedded to God. In the *Zohar, Keneset Yisra’el* can refer to the earthly community of Israel but also (often primarily) to *Shekhinah*, the divine feminine counterpart of the people, the aspect of God most intimately connected with them. On this harvest festival, She unites with Her partner, *Tif’eret*, the blessed Holy One. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-96b|3:96b]]–98b.

[^482]: **other powers and chariots below, dividing plunder…** The heavenly powers appointed over the nations of the world. On the seven days of the festival of *Sukkot*, they feed on seventy bullocks offered by Israel; then on the eighth day Israel rejoices alone with God.

    See [[Torah 41 - Pinchas#^29-12|Numbers 29:12–34]]; BT *Sukkah* 55b; *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:23; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 28:9; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64a|1:64a]]–b; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1251–53.

[^483]: ***Time to act for YHVH*…This has been established** See *Sifrei Zuta* 27:1; BT *Berakhot* 54a, 63a; *Midrash Mishlei* 5:16; *Midrash Tehillim* 119:57; *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-128a|3:128a]] (*IR*).

[^484]: ***time* is Assembly of Israel** *Shekhinah* is constantly changing, influenced by various forces at various times. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-194a|1:194a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-155b|2:155b]].

[^485]: **What does this mean:… *at just any time?… from a strange woman*** The shadow side of the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*, is the demonic feminine, the *strange woman*. If the priest enters the Sanctuary improperly, he empowers this evil power.

[^486]: **corresponding to: *They offered strange fire in the presence of YHVH*** Aaron’s two eldest sons, Nadab and Abihu, were killed when *they offered strange fire in the presence of YHVH* ([[Torah 26 - Shmini#^10-1|Leviticus 10:1–2]]). According to the *Zohar*, the *strange fire* symbolizes the demonic feminine. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73b|1:73b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 37 (44).

[^487]: **drawing near, being illumined, uniting fittingly** The various stages in the union of *Shekhinah* and *Tif’eret*.

[^488]: ***As for me, may my prayer come to You, O YHVH, at a time of favor*** The verse describes the approach of *Shekhinah*, known as *prayer*, to *Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*.

[^489]: **דוד‎ ויעש (*Va-ya’as David*), *David made, a name*…** To Rabbi Yehudah, the verse implies that King David enhanced *Shekhinah*, the divine name, actualizing Her. Similarly, one who studies Torah arrays *Shekhinah*, “making” Her ready to unite with *YHVH*, the blessed Holy One. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|1:142b]]; [[Zohar 8.06 - Parashat Be-Huqqotai#^z3-113a|3:113a–b]].

[^490]: **there would never have been separation from the blessed Holy One** Both Israel and their divine counterpart, *Shekhinah*, would have always been united with the blessed Holy One.

[^491]: ***I, YHVH, will hasten it*…** Hasten the redemption.

[^492]: **What is בעתה (*be-ittah*)*?* ה‎ בעת (*Be-et he*)…** *Shekhinah* is symbolized by the final letter of the name יהוה (*YHVH*).

[^493]: **Assembly of Israel lies in the dust for one day, no more!** The exile will last for a thousand years, comprising one divine day. See [[Psalms 090#^90-4|Psalms 90:4]]: *For a thousand years in Your eyes are like yesterday that has passed*.

    See *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:40; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Toledot* 4; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* (Friedlander) 28, (Higger) 27; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-17a|2:17a]], 227b; 3:270a; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.09 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^zh-28c|28c]] (*MhN*).

[^494]: **ה (*he*) separating from ו (*vav*)** The last two letters of the name יהוה (*YHVH*) symbolize *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*, whose union is ruined by the destruction of the Temple (“Her realm”) and the ensuing exile. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]].

[^495]: **because *vav* departed from *he* and voice disappeared…** The rupture of the divine couple separated the voice (*Tif’eret*) from speech (*Shekhinah*).

[^496]: **that entire day of he. What is that? Fifth millennium** The exile of *Shekhinah* extends throughout the fifth millennium, symbolized by Her letter, ה (*he*), whose numerical value is five.

    According to traditional Jewish chronology, the world was created less than 6000 years ago, at a date ostensibly corresponding to 3761 B.C.E. The fifth millennium corresponds to 240/41 C.E.–1239/40 C.E.

[^497]: **although She went into exile before the beginning of the fifth millennium** The Temple in Jerusalem was destroyed in the year 70 C.E., 170 years before the beginning of the fifth millennium; so Her exile, extending throughout the fifth millennium, lasts more than a thousand years.

[^498]: **When the sixth millennium arrives—mystery of *vav*** The sixth millennium, symbolized by the letter ו (*vav*), whose numerical value is six, begins in the year 1240/41 C.E. See below, page 188; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]]-10a; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 12.11 - Zohar al ha-Parashot#^zh-56b|56b-c]].

[^499]: ***vav* will raise *he* at six times ten** *Tif’eret* will raise *Shekhinah* from the dust of exile in the sixtieth year of the sixth millennium, i.e., in 1299/1300 C.E. This prediction of the beginning of redemption points to a date soon after the appearance of the *Zohar*.

[^500]: **ascending to ten, descending to five…** *Tif’eret*, symbolized by *vav*, equivalent to six, ascends to *Ḥokhmah*, symbolized by the י (*yod*) of יהוה (*YHVH*), equivalent to ten. Empowered by this ascent, He then descends to *Shekhinah*, symbolized by *he*, equivalent to five, and raises Her.

[^501]: **In the six hundredth year of the sixth** Corresponding to 1839/40 C.E.

[^502]: **to enter the seventh… to enter Sabbath** The seventh millennium, the cosmic Sabbath, beginning in 2240/41 C.E. In M *Tamid* 7:4, the Messianic era is described as “the day that is entirely Sabbath.”

[^503]: **one day of exile for Assembly of Israel, no more…** See above, note 493.

[^504]: **duration of the world and the days of Creation…** As the six days of Creation were followed by Sabbath, so the world will exist for six thousand years followed by a millennial Sabbath. See BT *Sanhedrin* 97a: “Rabbi Katina said, ‘The word will exist for six thousand years and for one thousand lie desolate.’”

[^505]: **Then the rainbow will appear… in radiant colors, like a woman…** The rainbow symbolizes *Shekhinah*, adorned in sefirotic colors for Her husband, *Tif’eret*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-1b|1:1b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72b|72b]].

[^506]: ***I will see her, to remember the everlasting covenant*…** God’s promise to Noah after the Flood: *When the bow is in the clouds, I will see it* \[or: *her*\], *to remember the everlasting covenant*.

[^507]: **Who is *everlasting covenant*? Assembly of Israel** *Shekhinah* shares the designation *covenant* with *Yesod*.

[^508]: ***Vav* will join *he*** *Tif’eret* will join *Shekhinah*.

[^509]: **Reubenites will instigate wars throughout the world** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-235b|1:235b]]–236a.

[^510]: **for a count of *vav*, six times ten, ten years** For the first sixty years of the sixth millennium.

[^511]: ***The Book of Rav Yeiva Sava*** One of many volumes housed in the real or imaginary library of the author(s) of the *Zohar*. It is cited frequently (1:47b, 79b; 2:6a, 60b, 206b; 3:7b, 155b, 289a, 290a, 295a \[*IZ*\]). See Matt, *Zohar*, 25; and the comment by Shim’on Lavi, *KP*, 1:12d: “All such books mentioned in the *Zohar*… have been lost in the wanderings of exile….Nothing is left of them except what is mentioned in the *Zohar.”*

[^512]: ***Then the land will desire*** The verse reads: *Then the land will desire* \[or: atone for, make up for, accept\] *its Sabbaths. The land* symbolizes *Shekhinah*.

[^513]: **mystery of *vav*, as is written… *with* יעקוב (*Ya’aqov*), *Jacob*…** In the entire Torah, only here is the name יעקוב (*Ya’aqov*), *Jacob*, spelled with a ו (*vav*), alluding to the messianic calculation noted above, page 180, and to *Tif’eret*, partner of *Shekhinah*. *Vav* is numerically equivalent to six, and *Tif’eret* includes within Himself all six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*, “all as one.”

    See Rashi and *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc.; below, page 188; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]]-10a; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-66b|3:66b]].

[^514]: **the land will be desirable to the blessed Holy One** *Shekhinah*, symbolized by *the land*, will be desirable to Her partner, *Tif’eret*.

[^515]: **day of which the Companions have spoken** The thousand-year-long day of exile. See above, note 493.

[^516]: **all discovered in the mystery of letters of the Holy Name** As noted above, pages 179–81.

[^517]: **Rav Yeiva Sava revealed the exile through those letters** Through the unique spelling of יעקוב (*Ya’aqov*), *Jacob*.

[^518]: **through these letters it is revealed** Through the letters of the name יהוה (*YHVH*).

[^519]: **He said to him** Rabbi Yehudah said to Rabbi Yose.

[^520]: **by this rung** By *Shekhinah*. See above, p. 174 and n. 454.

[^521]: **only through the mystery of *vav*…** Through the power of *Tif’eret*, symbolized by the *vav*, attached here to the name יהוה (*YHVH*).

[^522]: **embracing all, revealing all** *Tif’eret*, situated at the center of the *sefirot*, transmits the entire flow of emanation.

[^523]: **whatever is concealed reveals everything concealed…** *Tif’eret* is more concealed than *Shekhinah* and better suited to disclose what is hidden.

[^524]: **the blessed Holy One makes everything depend upon returning…** Turning back to God. See BT *Sanhedrin* 98a: “Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Levi pointed out a contradiction. It is written: *in its time;* yet it is written: *I will hasten it*. If they prove worthy, *I will hasten it;* if not, *in its time.”*

[^525]: **When shafts of fire strike the white of your hands** “Shafts” renders קולפין (*qulpin*), “poles, lances.” “White of” renders טהירי (*tehirei*), whose root means “bright”; here the word apparently refers to the palms.

[^526]: **entered a cave… found a book…** The motif recurs in *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-13a|2:13a]]–b; *ZḤ* 53c–d. According to legend, the *Zohar* itself was composed or hidden in a cave. See *OḤ*, intro; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:13, 19; Huss, “Hofa’ato shel Sefer ha-Zohar,” 528.

    According to rabbinic tradition, Rabbi Shim’on and his son, Rabbi El’azar, hid from the Roman authorities in a cave for thirteen years. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 79:6; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 10:9; BT *Shabbat* 33b; JT *Shevi’it* 9:1, 38d; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 11:16; *Midrash Tehillim* 17:13; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-11a|1:11a]]–b, [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-216b|216b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.11 - Parashat Ki Tetse#^zh-59c|59c–60a]].

[^527]: **seventy-two engravings of letters originally transmitted…** According to various medieval traditions, the angel Raziel transmitted a magical book to Adam. This book included “seventy-two species of wisdom” (*Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-55b|1:55b]]), corresponding to a complex divine name derived from the description of the splitting of the Red Sea: [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^14-19|Exodus 14:19–21]]. Each of these three verses contains seventy-two letters. The name is composed of seventy-two triads, according to the following pattern: the first letter of the first verse, the last letter of the second verse, the first letter of the third verse (forming the first triad); the second letter of the first verse, the penultimate letter of the second verse, the second letter of the third verse (the second triad); etc.

    On the seventy-two-letter name, see *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^14-21|Exodus 14:21]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 44:19; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 23:2; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:2; Hai Gaon, *Otsar ha-Ge’onim, Ḥagigah*, 23; Rashi on BT *Sukkah* 45a, s.v. *ani*; Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^14-19|Exodus 14:19]]; *Bahir* 79 (110); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7b|1:7b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-17a|17a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-51b|2:51b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-132b|132b]], 270a; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-150b|3:150b–151a]]; Trachtenberg, *Jewish Magic and Superstition*, 95–97; Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, 14:67, 284–86. (The work known as *Sefer Razi’el*—the Book of Raziel, comprising ancient magical, mystical, and cosmological teachings—was probably compiled in its present form in the seventeenth century.)

[^528]: **through which he discovered** Through which Adam discovered.

[^529]: **all those behind the millstones…** Demonic forces. The phrase derives from [[Torah 15 - Bo#^11-5|Exodus 11:5]]: *the slave girl who is behind the millstones*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-48a|1:48a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-177a|177a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-223b|223b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-28a|2:28a]], [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|37b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-191b|191b]]; and the description of the heavenly curtain in 2:259a (*Heikh*).

    “Dazzling demons” renders טהירין (*tehirin*), from the Aramaic root meaning “brightness, noon.” One class of demons is named טהרי (*tiharei*), “noonday demons.” See [[Psalms 091#^91-6|Psalms 91:6]] and Rashi, ad loc; *Targum*, [[Song of Songs 4#^4-6|Song of Songs 4:6]]; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 35 - Nasso#^6-24|Numbers 6:24]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-24|Deuteronomy 32:24]]. The Hebrew root טהר (*thr*), “pure,” lends this demonic name a euphemistic tone.

[^530]: **when a cloud will arise in the West, darkening the world** Apparently alluding to war.

[^531]: **sides** Pages.

[^532]: ***Abram journeyed… toward the Negev*… toward the South…** The Negev is the southern region of Canaan and, by extension, *negev* also means “south” in biblical Hebrew. Here it is identified with the warmth of *Ḥesed* (“Love”). Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham attained this rung.

    See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-111a|111a–b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^533]: **he did not attain his realm fittingly until Isaac was born** Abraham could not fully attain *Ḥesed* until the birth of Isaac, who symbolized the polar opposite, *Gevurah*.

[^534]: **joining with him, and they were linked with one another** Each balancing and fulfilling the other.

[^535]: **he—no one else—named him Isaac, so that water would merge with fire** So *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham and water, would merge with *Gevurah*, symbolized by Isaac and fire. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-103b|1:103b]]–104a, [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-119b|119b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|133b]].

[^536]: **Why *who was born to him?*** The phrase seems superfluous.

[^537]: **Fire from water** The phrase derives from *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:12. See above, page 159.

[^538]: **מצחק (*metsaḥeq*), *playing*** The ambiguity of the word invites interpretation, as we soon see.

[^539]: **he did not attain a name** From the time Isaac’s birth is predicted ([[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-1|Genesis 18]]) until after the death of Abraham ([[Torah 05 - Chayei Sara#^25-9|Genesis 25:9]]), Ishmael is not mentioned by name but is referred to simply as: *son of the maidservant, son of Hagar, his son, boy, lad* ([[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-9|Genesis 21:9–20]]). See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-9|Genesis 21:9]].

[^540]: **scoria** סספיתא (*Suspita*), “Dross, slag, scoria,” a neologism apparently based on כוספא (*kuspa*), “pomace, husk, residue.” Gold symbolizes Isaac and his corresponding *sefirah, Gevurah*, while the dross symbolizes Ishmael and the forces of evil left over after the refining process of emanation.

    See Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 104: “A king does not want to mention dross where silver is found.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30a|1:30a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-71b|71b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-228a|228a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-24b|2:24b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-203a|203a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-224b|224b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-236b|236b]]; Moses de León, *Sod Eser Sefirot*, 384; *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 182; Scholem, *Major Trends*, 389, n. 54; Liebes, *Peraqim*, 336–38.

    On the unfit nature of Ishmael, see *Sifrei*, [[Torah 52 - Vayeilech#^31-1|Deuteronomy 31]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:11; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 36:5; BT *Pesaḥim* 56a.

[^541]: **in praise of Sarah… מצחק (*metsaḥeq*), reveling, in idolatry** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 53:11: “Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai said, ‘Rabbi Akiva used to interpret this to \[Ishmael’s\] shame, but I interpret it praiseworthily…. Rabbi Yishma’el \[in parallel sources: Rabbi Akiva\] taught, “This expression of צחוק (*tsehoq*), ‘playing, revelry,’ refers solely to idolatry, as is said \[in the account of the Golden Calf\]: *They rose to revel* ([[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^32-6|Exodus 32:6]]). This teaches that Sarah saw Ishmael building idolatrous altars, catching locusts, and sacrificing them.”’”

    See Theodor’s note, ad loc.; *Sifrei*, [[Torah 52 - Vayeilech#^31-1|Deuteronomy 31]]; *Tosefta, Sotah* 6:6; *Shemot Rabbah* 1:1; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-20a|20a]] (*MhN*).

[^542]: **Israel arose in the will… before the world was created** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:1: “Six things preceded the creation of the world. Some of them were created and some of them arose in thought to be created…. Israel arose in thought.”

[^543]: **Therefore Abraham issued into the world** To engender Israel.

[^544]: **world was sustained for his sake** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:9, on [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-4|Genesis 2:4]]: *These are the generations of heaven and earth* בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*), *when they were created*. According to Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Korḥah, בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*), *when they were created*, is an anagram of באברהם (*be-Avraham*), “through Abraham,” indicating that the world was created for his sake.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-3b|1:3b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-105b|105b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128b|128b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|230b]]; [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-117a|3:117a]].

[^545]: **Abraham and Isaac arose but did not settle… until Jacob issued…** Abraham and Isaac symbolize the polar opposite *sefirot Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, neither of which is completely stable without the mediating influence of *Tif’eret*, symbolized by Jacob.

[^546]: **ותתע (*va-teta*), *and strayed*… תעתועים (*ta’tu’im*), *delusion*** Since the latter refers to idolatry, Rabbi Shim’on reasons that the former does too. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 30; Rashi on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-14|Genesis 21:14]].

[^547]: **the blessed Holy One did not abandon them…** Hagar and Ishmael. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-17|Genesis 21:17–19]].

[^548]: ***God has seen your suffering*** The verse actually reads: *YHVH has heard your suffering*. Several witnesses read similarly.

[^549]: ***God has heard the voice of the boy*—and not:…*your voice*** Having committed idolatry, she no longer deserved God’s attentiveness.

[^550]: ***There where he is*** The verse reads: *For God has heard the voice of the boy there where he is*.

[^551]: **Lower Court… Higher Court…** See *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^24-16|Deuteronomy 24:16]]: “The Higher Court punishes only from twenty years and higher, while the Lower Court from thirteen years and one day.”

    See [[Torah 37 - Sh'lach#^14-29|Numbers 14:29]] (where the Israelites over twenty years old are condemned to die in the desert); M *Niddah* 5:6; BT *Shabbat* 89b; *Tanḥuma, Qoraḥ* 3; *Bahir* 135 (195); *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-113a|2:113a]]–b, [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-186b|186b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248b|248b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-293b|3:293b]] (*IZ*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-20a|20a]] (*MhN*).

    According to rabbinic tradition, Ishmael was a teenager at this time. See *Tanḥuma, Shemot 1; Shemot Rabbah* 1:1; *Bereshit Rabbah* 53:13 (and Theodor’s note); Naḥmanides on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-9|Genesis 21:9]]; Baḥya ben Asher on [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-18|Genesis 21:18]].

[^552]: **corresponding to what is written: *there where he is*** Under the age of twenty. Cf. *Bereshit Rabbah* 53:14; BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* 16b.

[^553]: **from where does his punishment derive?** What causes his death, if the heavenly court imposes punishment only from the age of twenty?

[^554]: **if he is less than thirteen, he is culpable for the sins of his father** Though not culpable for his own sins, he can be punished for his father’s sins. See *Rut Zuta* 1:5: “Rabbi Ḥiyya son of Abba said, ‘Until the age of thirteen, a son is punished for his father’s sins; from then on, each person dies for his own sin.’” See [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^24-16|Deuteronomy 24:16]]; [[II Kings 14#^14-6|2 Kings 14:6]].

[^555]: **if he is older than thirteen** And still less than twenty.

[^556]: **that he may die innocent and be well rewarded in that world…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 25:1; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 7:23; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 6:2; BT *Ḥagigah* 5a.

[^557]: **What if he is guilty and has not yet reached the age of twenty?…** Since the heavenly court has no jurisdiction over him, who brought about his death?

[^558]: ***Some are swept away without judgment*** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:8; BT *Ḥagigah* 4b–5a; above, page 169; *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|2:196a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-249a|249a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-54b|3:54b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-59b|59b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-283b|283b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-291b|291b]] (*IZ*); *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-77c|ZḤ* 77c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^559]: **he encounters that Destroyer—unintentionally, above or below…** The teenage sinner encounters the Destroyer, without heavenly or earthly judicial procedure.

    See BT *Bava Qamma* 60a–b: “Rabbi Yosef taught: ‘What is the meaning of the verse *None of you shall go out the door of his house until morning* ([[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-22|Exodus 12:22]])? Once permission has been granted to the Destroyer, he does not distinguish between righteous and wicked.’”

    See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 11; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|1:63a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68a|68a–b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|69a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-102a|102a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-107b|107b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-113a|113a]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-182b|182b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-197b|197b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-204b|204b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|196a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-227a|227a]]; [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-54a|3:54a–b]]. Cf. 2:264b (*Heikh*).

[^560]: **את (*Et*), amplifying the meaning** Grammatically, the accusative particle את (*et*) has no ascertainable independent sense, but Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when et appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT *Pesaḥim* 22b; *Ḥagigah* 12a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|1:247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

[^561]: ***God has heard the voice of the boy there where he is*** In his present youthful state.

[^562]: **יעקוב (*Ya’aqov*), *Jacob*—spelled with a ו (*vav*)** In the entire Torah, only here is the name יעקוב (*Ya’aqov*), *Jacob*, spelled with a ו (*vav*).

    See Rashi and *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc.; above, page 181; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]]-10a; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-66b|3:66b]].

[^563]: **mystery of a rung of wisdom, realm of Jacob** The letter vav symbolizes *Tif’eret*, attained by Jacob. *Vav* is numerically equivalent to six, and *Tif’eret* includes within Himself all six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*.

[^564]: **by Jacob, in the mystery of *vav*, namely, the sixth millennium** Redemption will arrive in the sixth millennium, as indicated by the *vav*, symbolizing Jacob. The sixth millennium begins in the year 1240/41 C.E. See above, page 180; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]]-10a; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 12.11 - Zohar al ha-Parashot#^zh-56b|56b-c]].

[^565]: **six moments and half a time** This is clarified below. See [[Daniel 07#^7-25|Daniel 7:25]]: *They will be delivered into his hand for a time, times, and half a time*. In this verse עדן (*iddan*), *time*, is often interpreted as “year.”

[^566]: **threshold…** דדשא‎ עבורא (*Ibbura de-dashsha*), “Door-bolt,” inserted into sockets in the threshold. See BT *Eruvin* 102a.

    Sixty years into the sixth millennium refers to the year 1299/1300 C.E., a date soon after the appearance of the *Zohar*.

[^567]: **six and a half years** The “six moments and half a time” mentioned above. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-270a|3:270a]].

[^568]: **totaling seventy-three** Corresponding to 1312/13 C.E.

[^569]: **In sixty-six** Corresponding to 1305/6 C.E. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]].

[^570]: **King Messiah will appear in the land of Galilee** See *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 40 - Balak#^24-17|Numbers 24:17]]; *Aggadat Mashiaḥ*, in *Beit ha-Midrash*, 3:141; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-7b|2:7b]], [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-8b|8b–9a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-220a|220a]].

[^571]: **One star in the East will swallow seven stars in the North** The star in the East symbolizes the Messiah, who derives from *Tif’eret*, identified with the East. See [[Torah 40 - Balak#^24-17|Numbers 24:17]]; *Targum Onqelos* and Naḥmanides, ad loc.; JT *Ta’anit* 4:5, 68d; *Devarim Rabbah* 1:20; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Devarim*, add. 6.

    The stars in the North symbolize foreign forces deriving from the left side, identified with the North. See [[Jeremiah 01#^1-14|Jeremiah 1:14]].

[^572]: **souls will be depleted from the body** See BT *Yevamot* 62a: “Rabbi Assi said, ‘The Son of David will not come until all souls in the body have been depleted.’” “The body” is the heavenly treasure house of unborn souls.

    See Rashi, ad loc., and on BT *Avodah Zarah* 5a; *Bahir* 126 (184); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.028b|1:28b]] (*TZ*); 2:142a, 157a, 161b, 174a, 253a (*Heikh*); 3:152a; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-10b|10b–c]] (*MhN*).

[^573]: ***All the souls of the house of Jacob… numbered sixty-six*** Alluding to the sixty-sixth year of the sixth millennium, by which the souls of the heavenly treasure house will be depleted.

    The citation here conflates two verses. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-252a|3:252a]] (*RM*).

[^574]: **In seventy-three** Corresponding to 1312/13 C.E.

[^575]: **the blessed Holy One will pelt them with fire and hailstones** See Ezekiel’s description of God’s attack against Gog (38:22): *I will execute judgment against him with pestilence and blood; I will pour down torrential rain and hailstones, fire and brimstone upon him and his hordes and the many nations with him*.

[^576]: **until the years total one hundred** Until the hundredth year of the sixth millennium, corresponding to 1339/40 C.E.

[^577]: **ו (*vav*) will join ה (*he*)** *Tif’eret* will join *Shekhinah*. See above, page 181.

[^578]: ***they will bring all your brothers …as an offering to YHVH*…** The other nations will gather the dispersed of Israel and bring them as an offering to God in Jerusalem.

[^579]: **The Ishmaelites are destined… to incite…** See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* (Friedlander) 30, (Higger) 29; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-32a|2:32a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 12.11 - Zohar al ha-Parashot#^zh-56b|56b]] (*MhN*).

[^580]: **small *vav*…** While *Tif’eret* is symbolized by a large ו (*vav*), *Yesod*, emerging from Him, is symbolized by a small ו (*vav*). Together the two letters spell וו (*vav*). *Yesod*, the divine phallus, unites with *Shekhinah*, engendering new souls or, as here, renewing ancient souls that had issued previously from the heavenly treasure house and reincarnating them in new bodies.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18a|1:18a]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-9b|2:9b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-11a|3:11a]], [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-53b|53b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-74b|74b]].

[^581]: ***May the glory of YHVH endure forever*—uniting fittingly** Referring to the union of the divine couple.

[^582]: ***May YHVH rejoice in His works*—bringing them down to the world** Human souls are God’s *works*, engendered by the divine union.

    See *Bahir* 14 (22); Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 489, 504; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19a|19a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|33a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b–60a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85b|85b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 51; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 56 (69). Cf. Ibn Ezra on [[Psalms 001#^1-3|Psalms 1:3]].

    The verse from [[Psalms 104#^104-1|Psalm 104]] is cited in an eschatological context in *Tanḥuma, Aḥarei Mot* 2; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-259a|2:259a]] (*Heikh*).

[^583]: **enter the Sabbath** The cosmic Sabbath of Messianic time, “the day that is entirely Sabbath” (M *Tamid* 7:4). See above, page 180.

[^584]: **one day for the blessed Holy One alone** See [[Zechariah 14#^14-7|Zechariah 14:7]]: *There will be one day that will be known as YHVH’s*.

[^585]: **to couple fittingly** With *Shekhinah*.

[^586]: ***all who are inscribed for life in Jerusalem*** Together with *all who are inscribed for life*, namely, new souls.

[^587]: **totality of all rungs as one, with one another** אתה (*Attah*), *You*, refers to *Tif’eret*, who can be addressed directly in the second person, together with the *sefirot* surrounding Him (from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*). The third-person pronoun הוא (*Hu*), *He* (here translated as *are*), refers to the hidden upper triad of *sefirot: Keter, Ḥokhmah*, and *Binah*. מלכי (*Malki*), *My King*, refers to *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). So the phrase encompasses all ten *sefirot*.

[^588]: ***Command salvation for Jacob*—may all messengers** The plural “messengers” reflects the plural noun in the verse: ישועות (*yeshu’ot*), “deliverances, salvations.”

[^589]: **from the side of compassion, not from the side of judgment** Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, also known as *Raḥamim* (“Compassion”).

[^590]: **Those coming from the side of compassion never perform…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 50:2: “One angel does not perform two missions, nor do two angels perform one mission.”

[^591]: **Look at the angel who appeared to Balaam!…** See [[Torah 40 - Balak#^22-21|Numbers 22:21–35]] and *Bemidbar Rabbah* 20:13: “He was an angel of compassion, but to him \[Balaam\] he became an adversary.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-207b|3:207b]].

[^592]: **messenger of compassion…, while toward him…** Toward Balaam. The angel’s nature did not change; rather, his compassion toward Israel entailed judgment toward Balaam.

[^593]: ***Command salvation for Jacob*, for they are in exile** The people Israel, descended from Jacob, lie in exile, awaiting redemption.

[^594]: **Jacob was Glory of the Patriarchs** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 76:1: “Rabbi Pinḥas said in the name of Rabbi Re’uven, ‘… The chosen of the patriarchs is Jacob, as is said: *For Yah has chosen Jacob for Himself* ([[Psalms 135#^135-4|Psalms 135:4]]).’”

    In the *Zohar*, Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who harmonizes and completes the polar opposites *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, symbolized by Abraham and Isaac.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|1:133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-144b|144b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-152a|152a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-163b|163b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-171b|171b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-172b|172b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-180a|180a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-207a|207a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-23a|2:23a]].

[^595]: **the expression *It came to pass in the days of* denotes sorrow**… ויהי (*Vayhi*), *It came to pass*, is read as היה‎ וי (*vai hayah*), “there was woe,” or הי‎ וי (*vai, hi*), “woe, wailing.”

    See BT *Megillah* 10b: “Rabbi Levi, or some say Rabbi Yonatan, said, ‘This matter is a tradition handed down to us from the Men of the Great Assembly: Wherever it is said: ויהי (*Vayhi*), *It came to pass*, this denotes sorrow.’ … Rav Ashi said, ‘ויהי (*Vayhi*), *It came to pass*, sometimes denotes this and sometimes not, while בימי‎ ויהי (*Vayhi biymei*), *It came to pass in the days of*, always denotes sorrow.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 41(42):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14:1]]): “Rabbi Tanḥuma said in the name of Rabbi Ḥiyya the Elder and Rabbi Berekhiah said in the name of Rabbi El’azar, ‘This interpretation came to us from the Exile \[Babylon\]: Wherever it is written: בימי‎ ויהי (*Vayhi biymei*), *It came to pass in the days of*, this denotes distress.’… Rabbi Shim’on said in the name of Rabbi Yoḥanan, ‘Wherever it is written: ויהי (*Vayhi*), *It came to pass*, this denotes either distress or joy. If distress, there is none like it; if joy, there is none like it.’”

    See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 11:7; BT *Megillah* 11a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-16b|1:16b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-140b|2:140b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-167a|167a]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231a|3:231a]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-77a|ZḤ* 77a]] (*MhN, Rut*).

    The phrase “tinged with” renders טיפסי (*tifsei*), based on טופסא (*tufsa*), “frame, mold, form, configuration, pattern.” See *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 179–80.

[^596]: **lowest of all upper rungs… *Devarim Shekhinah***, last of the ten *sefirot*, is called *Davar*, “word” of God, since She conveys the divine essence. Davar means both “word” and “thing.” Here the plural, *devarim*, is cited from the verse in Genesis and applied to *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-1b|1:1b]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-191a|3:191a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-193b|193b]].

[^597]: ***I am not a man of devarim, words*** At the beginning of his career, Moses was *heavy of mouth and heavy of tongue* ([[Torah 13 - Shemot#^4-10|Exodus 4:10]]), not yet filled with the divine word, not yet intimate with *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-192b|1:192b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-25b|2:25b]].

[^598]: ***Elohim tested Abraham*, for the evil impulse came to accuse…** In rabbinic literature the name *Elohim* refers to *Din*, the divine attribute of Justice, while *YHVH* refers to *Raḥamim*, the attribute of Compassion. See *Sifrei*, [[Torah 50 - Ki Tavo#^26-1|Deuteronomy 26]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15; 33:3.

    In the *Zohar* the name *Elohim* has a wide range of meaning. Sometimes it refers to the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment”); sometimes to *Binah*, the Divine Mother, situated above *Din*; sometimes to *Shekhinah*, since She leans toward the left, *Din*. This multivalent name can also refer to various powers originating in *Din*. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-111b|1:111b]]; [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-96a|2:96a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8a|3:8a]], [[Zohar 8.06 - Parashat Be-Huqqotai#^z3-113a|113a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-200a|200a]].

    Here *Elohim* alludes to *Din* and to the evil impulse, a harsh manifestation of *Din* who appears outside the divine realm, *after… devarim*, the rung of *Shekhinah*. See BT *Sanhedrin* 89b: “*It came to pass after these *devarim*, things, that God tested Abraham*…. Rabbi Yoḥanan said in the name of Rabbi Yose son of Zimra, ‘After the *devarim*, words, of Satan…. Satan said in the presence of the blessed Holy One, “Master of the universe! You bestowed upon this old man the fruit of the womb at the age of one hundred! From his entire feast could he not offer a single dove or pigeon to You?” He replied, “… If I tell him, ‘Sacrifice your son before Me,’ he will do so immediately.” Immediately, *God tested Abraham. He said, ‘Take your son.’”* See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-11a|1:11a]].

    In this passage Rabbi Shim’on suggests paradoxically that the evil impulse is working for *Elohim*. His accusation is actually part of the divine plan, a mystical and psychological challenge to Abraham.

[^599]: **Isaac was already thirty-seven years old** See *Seder Olam Rabbah* 1; *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 24; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 23; *Seder Eliyyahu Zuta* 2; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 31; Theodor’s note on *Bereshit Rabbah* 55:4, 587–88; above, page 130; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|1:230b]].

[^600]: **he had to be encompassed by judgment …** Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham had attained the rung of *Ḥesed* (“Love”), but he was devoid of its complementary opposite: *Din* (“Judgment”). Being too one-sided, he now had to balance love with rigor in order to round out his personality and become a complete human being in the image of God.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|1:230b]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-257a|2:257a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-18a|3:18a]]; Moses de León, *Sod Eser Sefirot*, 372. On Abraham and *Ḥesed*, see [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]]; [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-111a|111a–b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^601]: **water was embraced by fire** The free-flowing love of *Ḥesed* was encompassed by the fervid power of *Din*.

[^602]: **to execute judgment, arraying it in its realm** By binding Isaac on the altar, Abraham manifested the quality of *Din* both on earth and in the sefirotic realm.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 55:1: “*It came to pass after these things that God* נסה (*nissah*), *tested, Abraham*. It is written: *You have given* נס (*nes*), *a banner, to those in awe of You*, להתנוסס (*le-hitnoses*), *to be unfurled*…. ([[Psalms 060#^60-6|Psalms 60:6]])—trial upon trial, elevation above elevation, to test them in the world, to elevate them in the world like a ship’s banner. Why to such an extent? *Because of truth. Selah* (ibid.)—so that הדין‎ מדת (*middat ha-Din*), the attribute of Justice, would be verified in the world.” As the midrash goes on to say, the case of Abraham proves that God does not reward or punish arbitrarily. The patriarch was rewarded for his extreme devotion in being willing to sacrifice his son.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 55:6; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 43; *Mekhilta, Baḥodesh* 9; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140a|1:140a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-18a|3:18a]].

[^603]: **water was completed by fire, fire by water** Father and son completed each other: Abraham by manifesting severe judgment, Isaac by submitting lovingly. Their interaction balanced the corresponding *sefirot* above. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-103b|1:103b]]–104a, [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-118b|118b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|133b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164b|164b]].

[^604]: **One was judged, one executed judgment** … Isaac submitted to Abraham, and together they enacted the sefirotic drama.

[^605]: **Therefore the evil impulse came to accuse Abraham…** See above, note 598.

[^606]: ***tested et Abraham*—*et* precisely!** Grammatically, the accusative particle את (*et*) has no ascertainable independent sense, but Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when et appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT *Pesaḥim* 22b; *Ḥagigah* 12a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|1:247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

    In the *Zohar* the word את (*et*) symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who comprises the entire alphabet of divine speech, from א (*alef*) to ת (*tav*). See the Christian parallel in [[Revelation 01#^1-8|Revelation 1:8]]: “I am *alpha* and *omega.”*

[^607]: **This is Isaac, for at that time he dwelled in low power** Isaac was destined to rise to the *sefirah* of high power (*Gevurah* \[“Power”\] or *Din*), but before he was bound on the altar he was unfulfilled, inhabiting the lower realm of *Shekhinah*, who derives from *Gevurah* and is indicated by *et*.

    See *Bahir* 131 (190). On *Shekhinah* as “low power,” see *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-11b|3:11b]].

[^608]: **crowned in his realm alongside Abraham** Having undergone severe judgment, Isaac manifests *Din* alongside Abraham, who manifests *Ḥesed*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|1:133b]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-257a|2:257a]] (*Heikh*).

[^609]: **Then division became apparent: water versus fire** The two patriarchs and their respective *sefirot* have completed one another, but they are not yet harmonized. Their dialectical relationship awaits synthesis.

[^610]: **until Jacob appeared and everything harmonized…** Jacob, the third and decisive patriarch, symbolizes the *sefirah* of *Tif’eret*, who balances the polar qualities of *Ḥesed* and *Din*, symbolized by Abraham and Isaac.

[^611]: ***He said, ‘Take your son’*** The verse continues: ‘—*your only one, whom you love, Isaac—and go forth to the land of Moriah, and offer him up there as a burnt offering on one of the mountains I will tell you of.’*

[^612]: ***Take Aaron and his son Eleazar*… coax with words** See *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ* 1; *Sifra, Tsav, millu’im* 1:2, 40d; *Sifrei*, Numbers 92, 141; *Bereshit Rabbah* 16:5; *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 10; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Qoraḥ* 2; Rashi on [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-15|Genesis 2:15]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-79a|1:79a]].

[^613]: ***Your son, your only one, whom you love, Isaac*…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 55:7: “*Your son*. He asked, ‘Which son?’ He told him, *Your only one*. He replied, ‘This is the *only one* of his mother, and that is the *only one* of his mother.’ He said, *Whom you love*. He asked, ‘Are there boundaries in the innards \[the seat of emotions\]?’ He said, *Isaac*. Why did He did not reveal it \[right away\]? In order to make him \[Isaac\] even more beloved in his eyes and to reward him for every single word.”

    See BT *Sanhedrin* 89b; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 22.

[^614]: ***to the land of Moriah… to the mountain of myrrh*…** The alliteration indicates that Moriah, site of the Temple, is the site of offering incense. See [[II Chronicles 03#^3-1|2 Chronicles 3:1]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 55:7.

    Abraham and myrrh are linked in *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 3:6; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 2. In *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-3b|3:3b]] (*RM*) myrrh is identified with *Ḥesed*, Abraham’s sefirotic site.

[^615]: ***On the third day*. They have already established this** In *Bereshit Rabbah* 56:1, various biblical parallels are cited.

[^616]: **since it is said: *He rose and went to the place*…** Since apparently Abraham had already reached the site.

[^617]: **why: *On the third day… he saw the place from afar*?** If Abraham had already arrived in the land of Moriah, how could he see it *from afar*?

[^618]: **Jacob, who issued from him; this is *the third day*** Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, third day of the cosmic week of Creation, the seven *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Shekhinah*.

[^619]: ***He saw the place from afar*, as is said: *From afar, YHVH appeared*…** The phrase *from afar* implies prophetic vision. Furthermore, the verse in Jeremiah alludes to *Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*, who emanated *from afar*, from the furthest realms. See Rashi on [[Jeremiah 31#^31-2|Jeremiah 31:2]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-5b|1:5b]]–6a.

[^620]: ***He saw the place*—Jacob…*He took one of the stones of the place*** Since in the later narrative *the place* is associated with Jacob, so here too.

    In rabbinic literature, המקום (*ha-maqom*), *the place*, is a name for God. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:9: “Rabbi Huna said in the name of Rabbi Ammi, ‘Why do we modify the name of the blessed Holy One, calling Him *Maqom*? Because He is the “place” of the world; His world is not His place \[i.e., God contains the world; the world does not contain God\].’” See Urbach, *The Sages*, 1:66–79.

[^621]: **from him** From Isaac, or from Abraham through Isaac.

[^622]: **What is so admirable about Abraham …** If Abraham foresaw that Jacob would issue through Isaac, then he must have realized that he would not have to really slaughter Isaac, so what is so great about the fact that he bound him on the altar?

[^623]: **consummating** Completing the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*.

[^624]: **now existing for him *from afar*…** Now Abraham did not focus on Jacob, since he was intent on carrying out God’s command to sacrifice Isaac and did not want to entertain suspicions about God’s failure to fulfill His promise: *Through Isaac, seed will be named for you*.

[^625]: **dim lens…** נהרא‎ דלא‎ אספקלריא (*Ispaqlarya de-la nahara*), “Speculum \[or: glass, mirror, lens\] that does not shine.” See BT *Yevamot* 49b: “All the prophets gazed through a dim glass \[literally: an *ispaqlarya* that does not shine\], whereas Moses our teacher gazed through a clear glass \[literally: an *ispaqlarya* that shines\].” Cf. [[1 Corinthians 13#^13-12|1 Corinthians 13:12]]: “For now we see through a glass darkly, but then face-to-face.”

    In the *Zohar, Shekhinah* is the dim lens, the *ispaqlarya* that does not shine on its own but rather reflects the other *sefirot. Tif’eret* is the clear lens, the resplendent speculum, the *ispaqlarya* that shines. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33b|1:33b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-183a|183a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-23b|2:23b]].

[^626]: **Why did the clear lens retract…** Jacob, symbolizing *Tif’eret* (the clear lens), was not yet born, so his *sefirah* did not yet appear on the rung of *Shekhinah* (the dim lens).

[^627]: **Further, so that he would go and be rewarded** Abraham did not see Jacob clearly so that he would proceed innocently with the binding of Isaac—and be rewarded for doing so.

[^628]: **when Jacob arrived, Abraham had already died…** Actually, according to bib-heal genealogy Abraham was still alive when Jacob was born. Isaac was born when Abraham was 100 years old; Jacob was born when Isaac was 60 years old ([[Torah 06 - Toldot#^25-26|Genesis 25:26]]), i.e., when Abraham was 160; and Abraham lived to the age of 175.

    See *DE, Haggahot Maharḥu, Zohorei Ya’bets*, and *NZ* on this passage; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|1:138b]].

[^629]: ***They came to the place… and Abraham built an altar there*** The verse continues: *and arranged the wood and bound Isaac his son and laid him on the altar on top of the wood*.

[^630]: **Although they… saw Jacob… another appropriate way** Although they envisioned Jacob, Abraham surmised that God planned some other way for him to issue into the world even after the slaughter of Isaac.

[^631]: ***‘My father!’* They have already established this** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 56:4: “*Isaac said to Abraham his father, ‘My father!’* Why *his father, my father?* So he would be filled with compassion for him.” See Theodor and Mirkin, ad loc.; Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^22-7|Genesis 22:7]], n. 88.

[^632]: **why didn’t he answer him at all?** Abraham’s only response was: *Here I am, my son*. Why didn’t he answer his son’s implied plea?

[^633]: ***‘Here I am, my son’*… compassion … transformed into judgment** Abraham, who normally symbolizes *Ḥesed*, declares that he has taken on the quality of *Din*, symbolized by Isaac, *my son*. See *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 24.

[^634]: ***Abraham said*** The full verse reads: *Abraham said, ‘God will provide Himself the sheep for a burnt offering, my son.’ And the two of them walked on together*.

[^635]: **now: *my son*, not a sheep** The verse reads: *God will provide Himself the sheep for a burnt offering, my son*. Rabbi Shim’on, drawing on the Midrash, takes this to mean that Isaac, *my son*, is now the intended sacrifice.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 56:4: “*‘God will provide Himself the sheep*… And if not, *the sheep for a burnt offering is my son!’ The two of them walked on together*—one to slaughter, the other to be slaughtered.”

    See *Targum Yerushalmi* (frag.), ad loc.; *Pesiqta Rabbati* 40; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 31; Rashi and Radak, ad loc.; Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, ad loc., n. 94.

[^636]: ***Behold, the valiant*—supernal angels. *Cried* at that moment** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 56:5: “Rabbi Ḥanina son of Yitsḥak said, ‘… As our Father Abraham stretched out his hand to take the knife to slaughter his son, the ministering angels wept, as is written: *Behold, the valiant cried outside….’”*

[^637]: **seeking to comprehend the word that is written: *He took him outside*** The angels *cried outside* because they were shocked that God would allow Abraham to kill his beloved son. The word *outside* recalls the divine promise to Abraham: *He took him outside and said: ‘Look now toward heaven and count the stars, if you can count them!’ Then He said to him, ‘So shall your seed be.’*

[^638]: ***‘Abraham! Abraham!’*—separated by a punctuation mark…** In the Masoretic text, the two occurrences of the name are separated by a vertical line.
