Rabbi Shim’on takes this to mean that now, having undergone this ordeal, Abraham was a new, fulfilled person. See above, pages 193–94; *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-138a|3:138a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187b|187b]].

[^639]: **Similarly, *Samuel! Samuel!*…** At his initiation.

[^640]: **But *Moses! Moses!*—unseparated…** The two occurrences of his name are not separated by a line. See *Shemot Rabbah* 2:6: “But *Moses! Moses!*—there is no separation. Why?… With all the other prophets, He broke off speaking with them, while throughout Moses’ life He never broke off.”

    See *Sifra, Vayiqra, dibbura dindavah* 1:12; BT *Sotah* 12a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-11b|2:11b]]-12a, [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-19a|19a]] (*MhN*); [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-106b|3:106b]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-138a|138a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-174a|174a]], [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187b|187b]]; *Minḥat Shai* on [[Torah 13 - Shemot#^3-4|Exodus 3:4]].

[^641]: **To arouse him with another spirit…** Arousing his compassion. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|1:59b]]–60a (*Tos*).

[^642]: **Isaac was sifted…** Although Isaac wasn’t sacrificed, by being bound on the altar he was purified and accepted by God as if he had been offered up. See *Tanḥuma, Shelaḥ* 14; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 31.

[^643]: ***Abraham raised his eyes and saw: here, a ram*…** The verse continues: *caught behind in the thicket by its horns*.

[^644]: **created at twilight** See the list of supernatural phenomena in M *Avot* 5:6: “Ten things were created on Sabbath eve at twilight: … Some say also… the ram of Abraham our Father.”

[^645]: **in its first year, as is said: *one lamb in its first year*…** The verse continues: *for a burnt offering*. The daily burnt offering in the Temple consisted of a lamb in its first year. Similarly, the ram substituted for Isaac was in its first year.

[^646]: **You say it was created at twilight? But Isaac was not yet in the world!** How then could the ram be less than one year old? It must have been as old as the world!

[^647]: **Rather, a force was ordained so that this sheep would appear…** The sheep (or ram) itself wasn’t created in the Beginning but rather an energy (or potential) that eventually materialized when a substitute for Isaac was required. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-201b|3:201b]].

[^648]: **לא (*lo*)—written with an א (*alef*) but read with a ו (*vav*)…** In the Masoretic text the word *lo* is written with an א (*alef*), meaning *not*, but is traditionally read with a ו (*vav*), yielding the opposite sense: *In all their affliction* צר‎ לו (*lo tsar*), *He was afflicted*.

    See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 14; BT *Sotah* 31a; *Tanḥuma, Vayishlaḥ* 10; *Shemot Rabbah* 2:2; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-219b|3:219b]]; Rashi, Radak, and *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc.

[^649]: **לא (*Lo*), with an א (*alef*), an extremely high realm…** Read this way, the word alludes to *Keter*, the primal and incomprehensible *sefirah*, known as אין (*ayin*), “nothingness,” and לא (*lo*), “no, not.” See David ben Judah he-Ḥasid, *Mar’ot ha-Ẓove’ot*, 259–61.

    Alternatively, the word is read here as: לא׳ (*la-alef*), *to the alef*, also alluding to the primal *sefirah*, symbolized by the first letter of the alphabet.

[^650]: **ולא (*ve-lo*)… written with an א (*alef*) but read with a ו (*vav*)** With an א (*alef*), the phrase means: *and not we ourselves*. With a ו (*vav*), it means: *and we are His*. See *Bahir* 96 (141), where the phrase is interpreted as: אנחנו‎ ולא׳ (*ve-la-alef anaḥnu*), *and we belong to the alef*, to *Keter*, the One.

    See Scholem, *Das Buch Bahir*, 105, n. 3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-183b|3:183b]].

[^651]: **He shares in that affliction, so how can you say: *saved them*?** If God participates in their exile, He has not yet saved them.

[^652]: **rather: *saved them*, already, for He is right there in that affliction…** By sharing their affliction, He has already guaranteed their salvation.

[^653]: **Whenever Israel is in exile, *Shekhinah* accompanies them…** See BT *Megillah* 29a: “Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai says, ‘Come and see how beloved are Israel in the sight of the blessed Holy One! Wherever they went in exile, *Shekhinah* accompanied them. When they were exiled to Egypt, *Shekhinah* was with them…. When they were exiled to Babylon, *Shekhinah* was with them…. And even when they are destined to be redeemed, *Shekhinah* will be with them, as is said: אלהיך‎ יהוה‎ ושב (*Ve-shav YHVH Elohekha*), *YHVH your God will return*, (*with*) *your captivity*. The verse does not read והשיב (*ve-heshiv*), *will bring back*, but rather ושב (*ve-shav*), *will return*. This teaches that the blessed Holy One will return with them from amidst the exile.’” See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 14.

[^654]: ***angel of His presence saved them*—*Shekhinah*** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|1:61a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-113a|113a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-166a|166a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187a|3:187a]]; *Mekhilta, Shirta* 3.

[^655]: **how can you say that he *saved them*?** If the angel accompanies them in exile, he has not saved them.

[^656]: **pledges of the blessed Holy One in exile…** *Shekhinah* and Her retinue of angels constitute the blessed Holy One’s pledge deposited with Israel, guaranteeing that He will save them, redeeming Her along with them. The verse is understood to mean: Because *Shekhinah* (*the angel of His presence*) was deposited with them, the blessed Holy One *saved them*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-241a|2:241a]]; [[Zohar 8.06 - Parashat Be-Huqqotai#^z3-114a|3:114a–b]]; *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-65b|ZḤ* 65b]] (*ShS*).

[^657]: ***I have remembered My covenant*, first** First, the blessed Holy One remembers *Shekhinah*, known as *My covenant*.

[^658]: **and then: *Here, the cry of the Children of Israel has come to Me*** Actually, this verse precedes the other.

[^659]: ***And I have also seen*** The verse continues: *how the Egyptians are oppressing them*.

[^660]: **amplifying the meaning to include…** לאסגאה (*Le-asga’ah*), “To increase,” a Zoharic rendering of the rabbinic Hebrew לרבות (*le-rabbot*), “to increase”—that is, to include, amplify, or widen the scope of meaning. In rabbinic hermeneutics the word גם (*gam*), “also, too,” amplifies the literal meaning of a biblical word or phrase. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:14; JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b.

[^661]: **another, first of all…** Another vision, foremost: the suffering of *Shekhinah*. The prooftext here (*God remembered His covenant*…) precedes both other verses.

[^662]: ***With Abraham*** The verse reads: *God remembered His covenant with Abraham, with Isaac, and with Jacob*.

[^663]: **The verse should read: לאברהם (*le-Avraham*), *with Abraham*** The biblical word ברית (*berit*), *covenant*, is often followed by the preposition ל (*le*), rather than את (*et*), both of which can mean *with*. See [[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^34-12|Exodus 34:12]]; [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^7-2|Deuteronomy 7:2]]; [[I Samuel 11#^11-1|1 Samuel 11:1]]; [[Isaiah 55#^55-3|Isaiah 55:3]].

[^664]: **But אברהם‎ את (*et Avraham*) implies Her union…** Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when et appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT *Pesaḥim* 22b; *Ḥagigah* 12a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|1:247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

    In the *Zohar* the word את (*et*) symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who comprises the entire alphabet of divine speech, from א (*alef*) to ת (*tav*). See the Christian parallel in [[Revelation 01#^1-8|Revelation 1:8]]: “I am *alpha* and *omega*.”

    Here Rabbi Yehudah interprets the triple occurrence of et as implying the union of *Shekhinah* with each of the patriarchs.

[^665]: ***Et Avraham*—Southwest** South symbolizes *Ḥesed*, Abraham’s *sefirah*, while West symbolizes *Shekhinah*. See BT *Bava Batra* 25a: “Rabbi Abbahu said, ‘*Shekhinah* is in the West.’”

[^666]: **יצחק‎ את (*et Yitsḥaq*), *with Isaac*—Northwest** North symbolizes *Gevurah*, Isaac’s *sefirah*.

[^667]: **יעקב‎ ואת (*ve-et Ya’aqov*), *and with Jacob*—single coupling…** *Tif’eret*, Jacob’s *sefirah*, who harmonizes the polar opposites (*Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*), attains complete union with *Shekhinah*.

[^668]: **Similarly, הארץ‎ ואת‎ השמים‎ את (*et ha-shamayim ve-et ha-arets*)…** The phrase השמים‎ את (*et ha-shamayim*), *the heavens*, indicates the union of *Shekhinah* (symbolized by et and night) with *Tif’eret* (symbolized by *the heavens* and day). Likewise, the phrase ואת‎ הארץ (*ve-et ha-arets*), *and the earth*, indicates the union of *Tif’eret* (symbolized by the letter ו \[*vav*\]) with *Shekhinah* (symbolized by *et* and *the earth*).

    See BT *Berakhot* 11b, where Rava and Abbaye indicate that light is mentioned in the evening prayers and darkness in the morning prayers, “in order to mention the quality \[מדה (*middah*), “quality, attribute”\] of day during the night and the quality of night during the day.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-12b|1:12b]], 259a (*Hash*); [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-162a|2:162a]]; [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-260b|3:260b]], [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-264a|264a]].

[^669]: ***He said, ‘Surely they are My people*…’…** This verse precedes the one expounded above by Rabbi Yehudah: *In all their affliction He was afflicted, and the angel of His presence saved them*.
